15. cilappatikāramum maṇimēkaliyum potuvaki kuṭi makkaḷik ilakkiyat taliviyarākak koṇṭavi; taṉit taṉmi vāyntavi. ivviru kāviyaṅkaḷiṉ kālam ki.pi. 350 - 450 ākalām eṉpar cuvelapil (1995). kāñcipurattil ki.pi. 5 ām nūṟṟāṇṭil vāḻnta tikñāṉar camaṟkirutattil niyāyappiravēca eṉṉum aḷavi nūli eḻutiṉār. annūlukkum maṇimēkaliyiṉ cila pakutikaḷukkum neruṅkiya oppumi iruppatāl maṇimēkaliyiṉ kālatti kipi 5ām nūṟṟāṇṭukkup piṉ taḷḷukiṟār viyāpuri. āṉāl maṇimēkaliyi āṅkilattil peyartta ālaṉ tāṉiyalō (Alin Danielou) ikkarutti maṟuttut tamiḻar aḷavi nūṟkaruttukkaḷiyē maṇimēkali āciriyarum, tikñāṉarum payaṉpaṭuttiyirukkalām eṉṟum maṇimēkali kipi. iraṇṭām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉatāka irukkalām eṉṟum karutukiṟār.
16. mēlē pattikaḷ 7, 8l kuṟitta aṟiñarkaḷiṉ tol kāppiyam kaḻaka ilakkiyac cevviyal taṉmic ciṟappup paṟṟiya āṅkila mūlaṅkaḷ varumāṟu; piṟa moḻiyāḷarukku ikkaruttuk kaḷi āṅkilattil terivikka ēntāka īṇṭu orē iṭattil tarappaṭukiṉṟaṉa :
As a source book not only of grammatical and literary study but also of human goography, social anthropology, culture, ecology and psychology, Tholkappiam contins very valuable data, and its importance for the study of Tamil culture, and of cultures in general, can hardly be exaggerated. However one must beware of taking the literary conventions as a kind of direct report, or even worse, as an objective explicit and ‘scientific treatment of 'real life'. In some works it was assumed that the bardic court-poetry reflected directly and with utmost fidelity and realism the conduct of men and women of that age in love and war. But the poetry, based on conventions, reflects “real life' only obliquely, since a literary convention was a pattern of fictional (or 'ideal') behaviour. The poets and the theoreticians had a clear understanding of the relationship between “Real life and its reflection in literature, since they clearly differentiated between the two: ulakiyal valakku denoted things sid and done in ‘real life’: Ceyyul valakku (or pulaneri valakku) denoted the practice in poetry of reflecting it. A literary convention is an agreement between writer and reader that certin themes will be represented in a certin way. The reader is thus truly able to appreciate the poet's skill in handling a familiar theme by means of improvisation. The poetry of the 'interior' the subjective akam poetry is totally anonymous in the sense that akam poems never mention the hero or heroine by name, not in a single stanza. Hence they“embody what is typically human rather than what is merely individual and particular W.H.Hudson. An introduction to the study of literature, London. 1946.p97). The objective external Puram poetry is partly impersonal. partly personal. Thus, e.g. the elegies are highly personal tributes to dead patrons and friends, genuine and spontaneous, expressing intimate and personal grief, “free from any conventional bucolic machinery”
- K.V. Zvelebil (1974) Tamil Literature (in series: A History of Indian literature) Otto Harrassowitz. Wiesbaden. pp 35-36
“The discovery of this body of roughly 2300 verses (of Ettuthogi and Pathuppattu, during 1880-1910) rised the Tamil language at one stroke, from an insignificant neo-Indian Language to the standing of one of the great classical languages of the world. Whoever has made a more intimate acquaintance with this ancient poetry, will readily subscribe to the assessment of the French Indologist Pierre Meille, when he states that this poetry is comparable with the choicest gems of old Greek lytical poetry, and that this “cycle of Sangam poetry' represents one of the summits of literary creation in India and the whole world'
- K.V.Zvelebil (1960) New Orient (bi-monthly) 5, 1960pp 3. reprinted in Tamil Culture x-2: April-June 1963.
“The Dravidians, and in particular, the Tamils have contributed a great deal to the cultural richness of the world: Pallava and Chola temple architecture, Chola bronze sculpture, the dance form known as Bharatanatyam the so called Carnatic system of music. But proba bly the most significant contribution is that of Tamil literature. which still remins to be “discovered” and enjoyed by the non-Tamilians and adopted an an essential and remarkable part of universal heritage”
- K.V. Zvelebil (1973) The smile of Murugan--on Tamil literature of South India; Leiden: E.J.Brill. Preface
“The early Tamil poetry was rather unique not only by virtue of the fact that some of its features were so unlike everything else in India, but by virtue of its literary excellence, those 26350 lines of poetry promote Tamil to the rank of one of the great classical languages of the world--though the world at large only just about begins to realise it.
“it is only the Tamil culture that has produced - uniquely so in India, an independent indigenous literary theory of a very high standard, including metrics and prosody, poetics and rhetoric”
“there exist in India only two great specific and independent classical and historically attested cultures--the Sanskrit culture and the Tamil culture.”
“Tamil literature is the only Indian literature which is both classical and modern; while it shares antiquity with much of Sanskrit literature, and is as classical, in the best sense of the word as for example the ancient Greek poetry, it continues to be vigorously living modern writing of our days.
"Classical Tamil literature is literature about and of people. but not a Volksliterature"
- K.V. Zvelebil (1973) The smile of Murugan; pp 1, 4
"Life is condidered pleasent and joyful, death an inevitable end. There is no attempt to obtin release from life. Liberality and goodness are indulged in without motives of penance or recompense. About old age thereis a note of nostalgic resignation.
“Life after death is represented as an abode of permanent happiness for the brave and good. and permanent suffering for the wicked. The happiness of future life is a reward of those who by their bravery and altruism established their glory (pukal) in this world. Love and courtship, marriage and children are considered necessary modes of personal perfection. If at all anything remins after death, it is the glory and honour, the prise due the memory of the days passed in heroism and in the service of fellow men.”
- K.V.Z. (1974) Tamil literature otto Harrassovitz, p.44.
“Ancient Tamil poetry is multivalent: it is so rich and comprehensive that it includes aesthetic structures and values which give high satisfaction to later ages and periods. It is conceived so that rather a community than a single individual could and can realize all its strata and systems. That is why it has survived generational tastes, and has acquired a permanent and universal position"
- K.V.Z (1974) ibid pp 35-36
In the depth and width of erotic experience and in the conceptual framework of the entire phenomenal world, perceived as akam and puram, Tamil culture is unique andoriginal. And as. for the parallels between Tamil and Sanskrit literature-why must it always be presumed that it was the Tamil works that borrowed from Aryan?”
- K.V. Zvelebil (1975) Tamil Literature (annals of Tamil literature, abstining from value judgements) Leiden: E.J.Brill. p107
The ideal modern poem is expected to be objective, precise, organically complex and as well written as prose. Free from cliches an straddled adjectives, it should have the prose virtues of simplicity and hardness in a language charged with meaning to the utmost possible degree. XXxx A poem of indirection, yielding more than one layer of meaning, is a product of excellent craftsmanship. Tolka-- ppiyar's insistence on the three aspects nokku, payan and eccam, is clearly indicative of his predilection for a poetry that works subtly, indirectly and sugge stively. Nokku demands every syllable, every word and every line to be in harmony with the poem as a whole. By payan is meant the implicit statement on the purpose of an object. Though eccam which is pf two types--colleccam and kurippeccam - interpreted in diverse ways by the commentators, it is used to avoid explicit complete statements and to exploit the suggestive potential of the minimum number of words employed in the poem.
The concept of poetry as expressed in Tolhappiyar's Porulathikaram and the practice of Sangam poets as evidenced in the still cherished anthologies would reveal that the ancient Tamils had a poetics of the lyric which Elder Olson was looking for but could not find in the well known languages of the world. (Elder Olson:1962 “Sailing to Byzantium; Prolegomena to a poetics of the lyric” in Willbur Scot (ed) FIVE APPROACHES OF LITERARY CRITICISM; London-Macmillan.)
- P. Marudanayagam; CHEMMOZHI I - 2 Jan-Mar 2007
“First, Tamil is of considerable antiquity. It predates the literature of other modern Indian languages by more than a thousand years. Its oldest work, the Tolkappiyam, contins parts that, judging from the earliest Tamil inscriptions, date back to about 200 BCE. The greatest works of ancient Tamil, the Sangam anthologies and the Pattupattu, date to the first two centuries of the current era. They are the first great secular body of poetry written in India, predating Kalidasa's works by two hundred years.
“Second, Tamil constitutes the only literary tradition indigenous to India that is not derived from Sanskrit. Indeed, its literature arose before the influence of Sanskrit in the South became strong and so is qualitatively different from anything we have in Sanskrit or other Indian languages. It has its own poetic theory, its own grammatical tradition, its own esthetics, and, above all, a large body of literature that is quite unique. It shows a sort of Indian sensibility that is quite different from anything in Sanskrit or other Indian languages, and it contins its own extremely rich and intellectual tradition.
“Third, the quality of classical Tamil literature is such that it is fit to stand beside the great literatures of Sanskrit, Greek, Latin, Chinese, Persian and Arabic. The subtlety and profundity of its works, their varied scope (Tamil is the only premodern Indian literature to treat the subaltern extensively), and their universality qualify Tamil to stand as one of the great classical traditions and literatures of the world. Everyone knows the Tirukkural, one of the world's greatest works on ethics; but this is merely one of a myriad of major and extremely varied works that comprise the Tamil classical tradition. There is not a facet of human existence that is not explored and illuminated by this great literature.
“Finally, Tamil is one of the primary independent sources of modern Indian culture and tradition. I have written extensively on the influence of a Southern tradition on the Sanskrit poetic tradition. But equally important, the great sacred works of Tamil Hinduism, beginning with the Sangam Anthologies, have undergirded the development of modern Hinduis. Their ideas were taken into the Bhagavata Purana and other texts (in Telugu and Kannada as well as Sanskrit), whence they spread all over India. Tamil has its own works that are considered to be as sacred as the Vedas and that are recited alongside Vedic mantras in the great Vaishnava temples of South India (such as Tirupati). And just as Sanskrit is the source of the modern IndoAryan languages, classical Tamil is the source language of modern Tamil and Malayalam.
“The status of Tamil as one of the great classical languages of the world is something that is patently obvious to any one who knows the subject" - George L. Hart (quoted by V.C. Kulandiswamy 2005)

iyal 4
cemmoḻit tamiḻ nūlkaḷip
piṟamoḻikaḷil peyarttal
1. nam maṉattiluḷḷa eṇṇattip piṟarukkut terivip pataṟkup pēcukiṟōm (orē moḻiyil pēciṉālum yāriṭam pēcukiṟōmō (naṇpar kuḻanti tāttā - pāṭṭi āciriyar mutalāḷi toḻilāḷi) ataṟkut takkavāṟu pēccu naṭi amikiṟatu. ituvē oru vakiyil moḻi peyarpputtāṉ eṉpār ākṭēviyō pās (Octovio Paz: "actual act of speech is a translation of thought') āyiṉum ivviyalil karutappōvatu oru moḻi nūlkaḷip piṟamoḻikku iṉṉārukku eṉṟu kuṟippāka illāmal aṉivarukkumāka peyarppatu paṟṟiyatu.
2. ilakkiyamallāta piṟa nūlkaḷi enta moḻiyil liruntum enta moḻikkum peyarppatil perum iṭarppāṭu illi. irumoḻikaḷilum pulamiyum nūl nutaliya poruḷ kuṟitta aṟivum iruntāl pōtum. āyiṉum ivaṟṟil kūṭa nām potuvākak kāṇum moḻi peyarppukaḷ taramāṉavi yāka iruppatilli. itaṟkuk kāraṇam kāci maṭṭum kuṟikkōḷākak koḷvatē eṉpār cema reṉyi (Szemerenyi: “unable to understand the text, but nevertheless translate for filthy lucre'))
3. ilakkiyaṅkaḷip piṟamoḻikaḷil peyarppatu cikkalāṉa paṇi:
(1) orē moḻik kuṭumpattic cārnta moḻikaḷiṭiyē ippaṇi caṟṟu eḷitu. (e.kā: tamiḻ - maliyāḷam, tamiḻ > kaṉṉaṭam teluṅku) 
(ii) piṟamoḻik kuṭumpattic cārntatāyiṉum moḻi yiyal taṉmikaḷil potumik kūṟukaḷ koṇṭa pakuti nāṭu (Linguistic area); nākarika paṇpāṭṭu orumippāṭu koṇṭa pakuti / nāṭu eṉṟu varum poḻutu moḻipeyarppu avvaḷavu kaṭiṉamalla (e.kā: tamiḻ -> inti marātti vaṅkāḷi) 
(iii) ēṉiya vakip piṟamoḻikaḷil ilakkiya moḻi peyarppuk kaṭiṉamāṉatutāṉ; ataṟkut taṉittiṟami vēṇṭum. 
atuvum kaḻaka ilakkiyam pōṉṟa 2000 āṇṭut toṉmi vāynta cevviya ilakkiyattip piṟa moḻiyil peyarppatu mēṟkaṇṭa mūṉṟu nilikaḷilumē kaṭumiyāṉatu tāṉ. tamiḻ - āṅkila moḻi peyarppup paṟṟiya eṭuttuk kāṭṭukaḷ maṭṭumē ivviyalil tarappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. āyiṉum tamiḻilakkiyatti āṅkilamallāta piṟa moḻikaḷil peyarp pataṟkum aṭippaṭik koḷkikaḷ poruntuvaṉavēyām.
4. ciṟanta putiṉam eḻutak kiyēṭu oṉṟum illi . atupōlac ciṟanta ilakkiya moḻipeyarppukkum kiyēṭu illi. eṉiṉum ilakkiya nūlkaḷi, kuṟippākac ceyyuḷ nūlkaḷi moḻipeyarkkum poḻutu piṉvarum aṟiñar kūṟṟukkaḷ karuttiṟ koḷḷat takkaṉa :
''mūlattil uḷḷati varikku vari appaṭiyē moḻi māṟṟa vēṇṭiyatilli. mūlattiṉ pāviya uṇarvu, uvamikaḷ - uruvakaṅkaḷ, pōṉṟavaṟṟiṉ, uyirōṭṭam moḻipeyarppil varavēṇṭum; ciṉṉañciṟu ceytikaḷi viṭṭu viṭṭālum kūṭa ''
- cuvelapil (1956): "caṅka ilakkiyap pāṭalkaḷi moḻi peyarttal - cila yōcaṉikaḷ" (ār. kirim keyt (1971) m collukkuc col peyarppatu ciṟanta moḻi peyarppit tarātu eṉpati viḷakkiyuḷḷār)
"moḻiyaṟivu vēṟu, ilakkiya uṇarvuvēṟu. ilakkiya uṇarvu iruntāl tāṉ uyiruḷḷa moḻipeyarppu varum. piṟamoḻi nūlic ciṟanta moḻi peyarppiṉ (vaḻi) mutalil paṭikkum māṇavaṉ, aṭuttāṟ pōl ammoḻiyiyum kaṟka viḻiyak kūṭum. (māṟāka oru moḻiyik kaṟkat toṭaṅkum māṇavaṉ ammoḻi ilakkiyattiyum paṭikkum aḷavukkut taṉ muyaṟciyit toṭarum vāyppu kuṟivu) ... ciṟanta eḻuttāḷaṉē ciṟanta moḻipeyarppāḷaṉ āka muṭiyum.
- kirikari rapacā (prāli : 1984 nūlil)
"moḻipeyarppu aṉittumē mūlattip pōṉṟa ''māyavaṭivi" (illusion) uruvākkum muyaṟciyē. cila cūḻnili kaḷil - moḻipeyarttōṉ tiṟamiyāl - mūlattip pōṉṟē māyavaṭivum amintuviṭa vāyppu uṇṭu. itu eṅku iyalum? mūlamoḻip pāviṉ vaṭivam karuttu ākiyavaṟṟukku ōraḷavu oṟṟumi uḷḷa vaṭivamum karuttum āṅkilattil irukkum nērvukaḷil tāṉ itu iyalum; attōṭu moḻipeyarttu eḻutiyatu eṉṟu koñcamum teriyāta aḷavukkuc ceyyuḷ amiya vēṇṭum.
- hikām (1938) 
''oru moḻiyil eḻutiya kaviti iṉṉoru moḻiyil moḻi peyarkkak kūṭiyatu aṉṟu. pāvalarkaḷiṉ paṭippu kaḷāltāṉ enta moḻiyum aḻiyāmal nilikkiṟatu. oru moḻiyil uḷḷa aṉittiyum atil uḷḷavāṟē moḻipeyarppilum koṇaramuṭintuviṭṭāl am moḻiyi varuntik kaṟka yār muyalvār? oru moḻikkē iyaṟkiyāṉa pā nayatti appaṭiyē vēṟu moḻiyil koṇara iyalātu eṉṟa nili iruppataṉāl tāṉ am moḻik kavitiyip paṭittu aṉupavikka ammoḻiyiyē nām kaṟka viḻikiṟōm.''
- ṭākṭar jāṉcaṉ 
"kirēkka ilakkiyatti moḻipeyarppu vaḻip paṭittup payaṉ illi; mūlatti ōraḷavu aṭiyāḷam kāṭṭavē moḻi peyarppāl iyalum.''
- varjiṉiyā ulp 
''kalviyiṉ orē nōkkam eṉṉa? hōmar kāviyaṅkaḷi mūla kirēkkattil paṭikka oruvaṉi āḷākkuvatē."
- ṣeppārṭu (1949) 
"hōmar kāviyatti "kirēkka vācakattukku iṇiyāṉa āṅkila vācakam" eṉṟavāṟu āṅkilappaṭuttiṉāl kāviyak karuttum miñcātu; naṭiyum miñcātu"
- ī.vi.riyu "ātici" āṅkila ākka muṉṉuriyil) 
"moḻipeyarppu eṉpatu eṉṉa? 
piṟa moḻippāvalaṉiṉ aṟupaṭṭatali - oru taṭṭil; 
kiḷiyiṉ pēttal; kuraṅkiṉ kūccal 
iṟantavaṉukku iḻivu. 				- viḷātimir nāpakāv
"moḻipeyarppu mūlatti muḻumiyākak kāṭṭa vallatalla eṉpatu aṉivarum aṟintatē eṉiṉum ulaka naṭappil ilakkiyappaṇikaḷil (taramāṉa) moḻipeyarp pukkum oru taliyāya iṭam uṇṭu"
- katē 
5. muṉivar vi. irāmacāmiyuṭiya "tirukkuṟaḷi . moḻipeyartta l"* (On Translating Thirukural: 2001) nūl kaḻaka ilakkiyatti āṅkilattil moḻi peyarppatil eḻum cikkalkaḷic cevvaṉē viḷakkukiṟatu; avaṟṟi iyaṉṟavari tavirppataṟkāṉa vaḻimuṟikaḷiyum tarukiṟatu. avar virivāṉa āyvukku eṭuttuk koṇṭa tirukkuṟaḷ āṅkila ākkaṅkaḷ varumāṟu:
1819 pirāṉcis viṭ ellis 
1840/1852 turu 
1886 ji.yu.pōp 
1930 va.ve.cu. aiyar 
1962 kē. em. pālacuppiramaṇiyam
1987 pi. es. cuntaram 
inta āṟu āṅkila ākkaṅkaḷiṉ niṟi kuṟikaḷi aṟiñar irāmacāmi virivāka āyvu ceytuḷḷatip paṭippatu tamiḻ -> āṅkila ilakkiya moḻi peyarppāḷarkkup perum payaṉtarum.
6. iṟutiyākap piṉvarum puṟanāṉūṟṟup pāṭalkaḷi, atāvatu
192. "yātum ūrē.'' 
252. " kaṟaṅku veḷḷaruvi '' 
271. ''nīraṟavu aṟiyā..'' 
159. "vāḻum nāḷōṭu.''
188. "paṭippup pala paṭittu.'' 
ākiya aintil ovvoṉṟukkum pōp, mār, irāmāṉujaṉ, hārṭ mutaliyōr ceyta palvēṟu moḻipeyarppukaḷiṉ niṟikuṟikaḷi pi. marutanāyakam (2003) kaṭṭuriyil nayampaṭa āyvu ceytuḷḷār atuvum kaḻaka ilakkiya moḻipeyarppukkuc ciṟanta vaḻikāṭṭiyākum. eṭuttukkāṭṭāka 192m pāṭalum ataṉ āṅkila ākkaṅkaḷum ippakutiyiṉ aṭiyil tarappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. avi kuṟitta kaṭṭuriyāciriyar karuttukaḷ varumāṟu: 
pōp cāṉaṭ (Sonnet) eṉṉum kaṭiṉamāṉa pāviṉattil, īṟṟetukikaḷuṭaṉ, mūlattiṉ poruḷ māṟāmal āṅkilap paṭuttiyuḷḷār. (īṟṟetukikkākaveṉṟē vantatu When joyous life seems like a luscious drught) eṉpa tu "vāḻtal iṉitu'' eṉpati "uyarkuṭippuk kuṭittuk kaḷittal pōṉṟa iṉiya vāḻkki" eṉṟu etukikkāka māṟṟiviṭṭār ātāramiṉṟi!)
mār: "tītum naṉṟum piṟar tara vārā" eṉpati " I receive Wrong doing as though it were good' (= tīmi ceyyappaṭṭālum ati nāṉ naṉṟākak karuti ēṟpēṉ) eṉa apattamāka māṟṟi viṭṭār. 'nōtalum taṇitalum avaṟṟōraṉṉa' eṉpati "Pain and relief from pin, are as one" (= nōvum oṉṟu tāṉ nōvu nīṅkuvatum oṉṟu tāṉ) eṉa āṅkilappaṭutti yuḷḷatum cariyalla (nōtal taṇital ākiyaviyum piṟar tara varuvaṉavalla eṉpatallavā poruḷ). "The cool drops of rin that fill with lightnings flash are not the same as it is” 6TOOTM toṭariyam pāṭaliṉ "miṉṉoṭu ..yāṟṟu' eṉṉum pakutiyip puriyāmal ceyta piḻi. kaṭici iraṇṭu varikaḷiṉ āṅkila vaṭivil foolish eṉṟa kaṭuñcolli āḷvatu mūlattiṟku etirāṉatu.
irāmāṉucaṉ: vaḻakkampōlac ciṟappākavē aṟputamāka āṅkilap paṭuttiyuḷḷār. puṟappāṭaliṉ karuttu muḻumiyāka uḷḷatu; nayamāṉa āṅkila naṭi; eṉiṉum oru ciṟukuṟi: ''ciṟiyōri ikaḻtal ataṉiṉum ilamē' eṉpuḻi ataṉiṉum eṉṉum colliṉ nalatti maṟantuviṭṭār. (pōp maṟakkavilli : Still less despise we eṉṟu kavaṉamāka eḻutiṉār!)
hārṭ & hipeṭs: hārṭṭiṉ tāymoḻi āṅ kilam ; tamiḻilum camaṟkirutattilum āḻnta pulamiyuḷḷavar; avarukku utaviyavar āṅkilap pāvalar hipeṭs. eṉavē ivviruvariṉ āṅkila ākkam muntiya mūvar ākkaṅkaḷi viṭac ciṟappākavuḷḷatu; mūlattiṉ poruḷi muḻumiyāka, teḷivāka appaṭiyē kāṭṭukiṟatu -- pānayamāṉatu pōp, irāmāṉucaṉ ākiyōr ākkaṅkaḷi viṭac caṟṟē māṟṟuk kuṟivāka iruppiṉum. āruyir = ariya uyir ; itaṉi "Our lives, however dear'' eṉṟār irāmāṉucaṉ ; a life with its hardships eṉṟār hārṭ ; pāṭaliṉ nōkkukku iccolli hārṭ moḻi peyarttatē poruttamāṉatu.
yātum ūrē, yāvarum kēḷir 
tītum naṉṟum piṟartara vārā 
nōtalum taṇitalum avaṟṟō raṉṉa; 
cātalum putuvatu aṉṟē ; vāḻtal 
iṉiteṉa makiḻntaṉṟum ilamē; muṉiviṉ 
iṉṉātu eṉṟalum ilamē; miṉṉoṭu 
vāṉam taṇtuḷi talii āṉātu 
kalporutu iṟaṅkum mallal pēr yāṟṟu 
nīrvaḻip paṭūum puṇipōl, ār uyir 
muṟivaḻip paṭūum eṉpatu tiṟavōr 
kāṭciyil teḷintaṉam ākaliṉ, māṭciyiṉ 
periyōri viyattalum ilamē 
ciṟiyōri ikaḻtal ataṉiṉum ilamē.
kaṇiyaṉ pūṅkuṉṟaṉ : puṟam 192 
To us all towns are one, all men our kin,
Life's good comes not from others' gift, nor ill; 
Man's pins and pins' relief are from within,
Death's no new thing; nor do our bosoms thrill 
When joyous life seems like a luscious drught.
When grieved, we patient suffer, for we deem 
This much prised life of ours a fragile raft
Borne down the waters of some mountin stream 
That O'er huge boulders roaring seeks the plin
Tho'storms with lightnings' flash from darken'd skies 
Descend the raft goes on as fates ordin.
Thus have we seen in visions of the wise! 
We marvel not at greatness of the great; 
Still less despise we men of low estate.
- G. U. Pope 191Q 
All villages are mine and every one my friend 
I receive wrong doing as though it were good, 
and do not pay it back. Pain and relief 
from pin are as one. To die is not new, 
while not to rejoice becuse life is sweet is 
absurd. Poor is the mind that holds it wrong 
to seek after something. The cool drops of rin 
that fall with the lightning's flash are not the same 
as it is. Like the waters of the stream that rushes 
among rocks is sweet life, set down on the pre-ordined 
path. Since this has clearly been foreseen by 
the wise it is foolish to extol the greatness of 
the great or to censure the lowly.
J. R. Marr 1958/1985 
Every town our home town, 
every man a kinsman 
Good and evil do not come 
from others 
Pain and relief of pin 
come of themselves. 
Dying is nothing new. 
We do not rejoice 
that life is sweet 
nor in anger 
call it bitter. 
Our lives, however dear, 
follow their own course
rafts drifting 
in the rapids of a great river 
sounding and dashing over the rocks 
after a downpour
from skies slashed by lightnings 
We know this 
from the vision 
of men who see. 
So, 
We are not amazed by the great,
and we do not scorn the little
- A. K. Ramanujan (1985) 
Every city is your city. Everyone is your kin, 
Failure and prosperity do not come to you becuse others 
have sent them! Nor do suffering and the end of suffering. 
There is nothing new in death. Thinking that living 
is sweet, we do not rejoice in it. Even less do we say 
if something unwanted happens, that to live is miserable!
Through the vision of those who have understood we know 
that a life, with its hardsdhip, makes it way like a raft 
riding the water of a huge and powerful river roaring
Without pasue as it breaks aginst rocksbecuse the clouds 
crowded with bolts of lightning pour down their cold 
drops of the rin, and so we are not amazed 
at those who are great and even less do we despise the weak
- G. L. Hart & Heifetz (1999) 
7. mēlē nāṉkām pattiyiṟ kaṇṭaṉavaṟṟiṉ āṅkila mūlaṅkaḷ iṉṟiyamiyātavi; avi varumāṟu:
“The detils are not so important; it is better to keep truly and devotedly the essence and nature of the poet's metaphors than to cling to trifles in a servile way and, at the same time, to lose the spirit and nature of the whole”
K. V. Zvelebil (1956) 
Language learning and the study of literature are two completely different things, and translation has to be a part of the latter if it is to receive the breath that is inherent to it. XXXXX a student is led into language study through literature and not the reverse. There are those who begin Greek after reading Aeschylus XXXXX
So, the translator must be a writer above all, with the instincts and drives that go to make a writer.”
- Gregory Rabassa in Frawley (1984) 
All translation is a kind of illusion, more or less perfect according to circumstances and varying also with the skill of the translator... Where there exists in English a corresponding form and manner a high degree of illusion is possible. Those translations are always best in which the illusion is most complete and the idiom least suggestive of translation"
Higham (1938) 
Poetry, indeed, cannot be translated, and therefore, it is the poets that preserve languages; for we would not be at the touble to learn a language, if we would have all that is written in it just as well in a translation. But as the beauties of poetry cannot be preserved in any language except that in which it was originally written, we learn the language."
- Dr Samuel Johnson
“ It is useless to read Greek in translation. Translators can but offer us a vague equivalent”
- Virginia wolf. 
“If we put Homer stright into English words neither meaning nor manner survives”
- E. V. Rieu (in his preface to his translation
of Homer's Odyssey)
 “The only purpose of education is to enable one to read Homer in the original Greek”
- Sheppard (1949)
 “What is translation? On a platter 
A poet's pale and glaring head 
A parrot's speech, a monkey's chatter 
And profanation of the dead.
- Vladimir Nabakov
 “Say what one will of its inadequacy, translation remins one of the most important worthwhile concerns in the totality of world affairs"
- Goethe

iyal 5
tamiḻiṉ toṉmi : piṟa aṟivup
pulaṅkaḷuṭaṉ iṇinta āyvu
(cintuveḷi nākarika akaḻvāyvu, annākarika
eḻuttāyvu uṭpaṭa)
tamiḻarkaḷ ulakil iṉṟuḷḷa maṟṟa enta moḻi, paṇ pāṭṭiyum viṭa atikat toṉmiyāṉatum iṭiyīṭu illātatum āṉa moḻi, paṇpāṭṭiṉ piṟaṅkaṭikaḷ āvar. mānta iṉa nākarika vaḷarcciyiṉ mikac ciṟanta iyalkaḷil oṉṟu tamiḻar marapuc celvam ākum. iṉṟuḷḷa moḻikaḷil māntaṉ toṉmoḻikku mika neruṅkiyatu tamiḻ maṭṭumē. "mutal tāymoḻi" āyvil īṭupaṭṭuḷḷa aṟiñarkaḷukku oḷi taravallatum tamiḻē. oru ciṟu nilapparappiṉ varalāṟu, paṇpāṭu ākiyavaṟṟic cariyākap purintu koḷḷa ippaṭi mānta iṉattiṉ oṭṭumotta iṉa, moḻi varalāṟu kaḷuṭaṉ toṭarpupaṭuttip pārkka vēṇṭiya niliyil uḷḷa (tamiḻakam pōṉṟa pakutikaḷ ulakil mikac cilavē; tamiḻ taṉit taṉmi vāyntatu. es ē ṭilar kūṟiyuḷḷatu pōl intiya nākarikattiṉ aṭittaḷam tirāviṭa (tamiḻ) moḻi - paṇpāṭu ākiyaviyē (S.A. Tyler. "India, m Anthropological perspective (1973) "All of Indian civilization is built on an underlying base of Dravidian language and culture') intiya varalāṟṟu āyvu kaḷukkum tamiḻ cārnta āyvukaḷ mika iṉṟiyamiyātavi.
2. tamiḻ eṉṉum colliṉ tiripē tirāviṭam (tamiḻ, tamiḷ, tramiḷa , trāmiṭa, trāviṭa). tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpam eṉa aḻippatu tamiḻiya moḻik kuṭumpattiyē. tol tirāviṭam (Proto-Dravidian) eṉṟu vaṇṇa ṉi moḻi nūlāyvāḷar mīṭṭuruvākkam ceyvaṉavaṟṟuḷ pala piḻiyāy muṭikiṉṟaṉa eṉpatum, paḻantamiḻē itaṟkuk kaṭṭaḷik kallākak koḷḷappaṭa vēṇṭum eṉpatum, pāvāṇar koḷki . tamiḻiṉ tiripukaḷē piṟa tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ. paḻan tamiḻiṉiṉṟum vēṟupaṭṭa "tol tirāviṭam' eṉpatu kaṟpaṉiyē eṉpar pāvāṇar. ākavē tirāviṭam, tol tirāviṭam eṉa innūlil kuṟippiṭappaṭuvaṉavellām uṇmiyil paḻantamiḻiyē kuṟippatākak koḷka.
3. tamiḻ, tamiḻar varalāṟṟic cevvaṉē aṟiya māntar iṉat tōṟṟamum paravalum; moḻiyiṉ tōṟṟamum paravalum paṟṟi iṉṟiya paṉṉāṭṭu aṟiñar ēṟṟuḷḷa karuttukkaḷit terintu koḷvatu iṉṟiyamiyātatu. avaṟṟi aṭuttu varum pakutikaḷil kāṇpōm. 
ka. mānta iṉattōṟṟamum paravalum
4. puṭaviyiṉ (pirapañcattiṉ) akavi cumār 1400 kōṭi āṇṭuvari irukkalām. ulakam uṭpaṭa ñāyiṟṟuk kuṭum pattiṉ akavi ēṟattāḻa 450 kōṭi āṇṭu ākum. ulakil mutaṉ mutalil uyirkaḷ tōṉṟiyatu ēṟattāḻa 300 kōṭi āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉartāṉ. mutalil tōṉṟiyavi 'pākṭīriyā' pōṉṟa ōraṇu uyirikaḷē. iṉṟuḷḷa aṉittuc ceṭi koṭikaḷum vilaṅku - paṟavikaḷum orē mūlattil tōṉṟiyaviyē ākum. ivvuyiriṉa vakikaḷil 15 ilakkam taṉi iṉaṅkaḷ (Species) kaṇṭu piṭikkappaṭṭup peyar cūṭṭappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. iṉṉum iṉam kaṇṭupiṭikkap paṭātaviyum, peyar cūṭṭappaṭātaviyum (kuṟippāka nilittiṇi, nuṇṇuyirkaḷ, ciṟṟuyirkaḷ) mummaṭaṅku irukkum eṉṟu aṟiviya lārkaḷ karutukiṉṟaṉar. iṉṟikku 24 kōṭi āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar oru taṭaviyum, āṟarik kōṭi āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar maṟṟoru taṭaviyum pēraḻivukaḷ ēṟpaṭṭu antantak kālakkaṭṭattil irunta uyiriṉaṅkaḷuḷ perumpālāṉavi aḻintuviṭṭaṉa. (mikap periya viṇ koḷḷikaḷ (Meteors) ulakil viḻuntatāl appēraḻivukaḷ ēṟpaṭṭirukkalām eṉkiṉṟaṉar). ippoḻutu uḷḷa pālūṭṭikaḷ (Mammals) aṉittumē 16 kōṭi āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar uruvāṉa oru ciṟiya eli pōṉṟa parumaṉuṭiya) vilaṅkiliruntu paṭimalarcci aṭintaviyē. kuraṅkukaḷukkum māntaṉukkum mūtāti yāṉa ilemūr (Lemur) vilaṅku (ēṟattāḻa 250 kirām eṭi) uruvāṉa kālam āṟarik kōṭi āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar ākum. (pūccikaḷ tōṉṟi 50 kōṭi āṇṭum eṟumpukaḷ tēṉīkkaḷ tōṉṟi 10 kōṭi āṇṭum, kaṟiyāṉ tōṉṟi 28 kōṭi āṇṭum ākiṉṟaṉa) ip pakkattiṟkum aṭutta pakkattukkum iṭiyil oṭṭiyuḷḷa 'puviyiṉ tōṟṟattiliruntu ikkāla māntaṉ vari' eṉṟa vivarap paṭatti kāṇka.
5. māntaṉ eppaṭit tōṉṟiṉāṉ? māntak kuraṅkiṉat tukkum (cimpaṉci, korillā) māntaṉukkum potuvāṉa vēṟu ōr uyiriṉam iṉṟikku ēṟattāḻa 50 ilakkam āṇ ṭukku muṉ āppirikkāvil iruntirukka vēṇṭum eṉpatu aṟiviyal muṭivu. iṉṟu ulakeṅkum uḷḷa 650 kōṭi māntarkaḷumē (atāvatu tirāviṭar, intō airōp piyar, maṅkōliyar, cemittiyar, amerikka intiyar ākiya aṉivarumē) hōmō cēpiyaṉs cēpiyaṉs allatu ikkāla māntar (Home Sapiens Sapiens or Anatomically Modern Humans) eṉṉum orē iṉattic cārntavarkaḷ. ivviṉam āppirikkāvil iṉṟikku oṉṟari ilakkam āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar vāḻnta orē tāyiṭam iruntu tōṉṟiyatu eṉpatu iṉṟiya aṟiviyalār aṉivarum ēṟṟa muṭivu (Cavalli Sforza and others: The history and geography of Human genes, 1994). ēṟattāḻa māntaṉiyotta "muṉmānta (Hominid) " iṉaṅkaḷ kaṭanta 48 ilakkam āṇṭukaḷil tōṉṟic cila pala ilakkam 1 āṇṭukaḷ vāḻntu piṉṉar muṟṟilum aḻintoḻintu viṭṭaṉa. avaṟṟuḷ hōmō erakṭas eṉṟa iṉamum aṭaṅkum. atu maṭṭumē āppirikkāvi viṭṭu veḷiyēṟip piṟa kaṇṭaṅkaḷilum paraviyiruntatu. kurōmakṉāṉ maṉitaṉ, pīkiṅ māntaṉ, cāvakamāntaṉ, attirampākkam pācil māntaṉ ākiyavarkaḷ (ēṟattāḻa 3 ilaṭcam āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar vāḻntavarkaḷ) inta hōmō erakṭs vakiyic cārntavarkaḷē. airōppāvil 40,000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉ vari vāḻntu piṉṉar aṭiyōṭu aḻikkappaṭṭuviṭṭa niyāṉṭartal (Neandarthal) iṉamum muṉmānta iṉamē. inta "muṉmānta iṉaṅkaḷ " evaṟṟiṭiyēyum "moḻi" uruvākavē illi (None of them had the faculty of speech)
6. ippoḻutuḷḷa māntarkaḷākiya AMH vakiyic cārnta nam mānta iṉam, kaṭanta oṉṟari ilakkam āṇṭu kaḷukku muṉṉar āppirikkāvil tōṉṟi ulakeṅkum piṉ varumāṟu paraviyatu - atāvatu,

(mikrōṉiciyā, pāliṉīciyā)
 paraviṉar eṉpatu vallunar karuttu ākum. iti viḷakkum nilappaṭam aṭutta pakkattil uḷḷatu. kaṇṭaṅkaḷ nakarvuk koḷkiyiṉ paṭi (Continental drift) kaṇṭaṅkaḷ kaṭanta 25 kōṭi āṇṭukaḷākap pirintu nakarntuḷḷaṉa. eṉiṉum ulakil iṉṟu uḷḷa kaṇṭaṅkaḷ ellām ēṟattāḻa ippō tuḷḷa uruvi ēṟattāḻa oru kōṭi āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉarē aṭintuviṭṭaṉa. ataṟkup piṉṉar, kaṇṭam aḷavukku (Continental proportions) peru nilappakuti etuvum kaṭalukkuḷ mūḻkavilli . āyiṉum paṉiyūḻi muṭivil ki.mu. 8000 vākkil (paṉikkaṭṭi urukik kaṭal maṭṭam cumār 300 aṭi uyarntatāl ulakeṅkum kaṭalōrap pakutiyākiya kaṇṭat tiṭṭu (Continental shelf) ēṟattāḻa - irunūṟu kal aḷavukku kaṭaluḷ mūḻkiyatu. avvāṟu akkālak kaṭṭattil tamiḻakattic cuṟṟiyum (teṉ tici uṭpaṭa) cila nūṟu kal (mil) tolivum kaṭaluḷ mūḻkiyirukkalām. avvāṟu mūḻkiya nilappakutiyiyē kaḻaka ilakkiyaṅkaḷum kaḷaviyal uriyum cuṭṭukiṉṟaṉa eṉpatē iṉṟiya aṟiviyalukkup poruntuvatākum. āppirikkāviliruntu ikkāla māntaṉ ēṟattāḻa 50,000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar āttirēliyā, mēṟku āciyā pōṉṟa pakutikaḷukkup pulam peyarntu ceṉṟatu teṉṉintiyā vaḻiyāka irukkalām. jiyākirāpikal mākacīṉ cepṭempar 2006 itaḻil ikkālamāntar āppirikkāvi viṭṭu teṉṉintiyāviyoṭṭiya kaṇṭattiṭṭu vaḻi yākap pulampeyarntu ceṉṟa pātiyik kāṭṭum nilap paṭam uḷḷatu. akkālak kaṭṭattil ippoḻutu uḷḷatu pōlavē ellāk kaṇṭaṅkaḷum iruntaṉa veṉiṉum nilap pakuti cila nūṟukal virivāka iruntirukkum. intiyak kari 


cārnta kaṇṭattiṭṭup pakutiyil atāvatu kariyōrak kaṭaṟ pakutiyil (Continental Shelf) āḻkaṭal akaḻāyvu ceytāl itu paṟṟiya cāṉṟukaḷ kiṭikkalām eṉṟu kūṟukiṟār piḷemiṅ (2004). iṉṟiya māntaṉiṭam uḷḷavi 23x2 'kurōmōcōmkaḷ. avaṟṟil aṭaṅkiya marapaṇukkaḷ (Genomes) eṇṇikki 30,000. namatu marapaṇukkaḷukkum māntakkuraṅku marapaṇukkaḷukkum 98.50 viḻukkāṭu oṟṟumi uṇṭu. (oṉṟari viḻukkāṭē marapaṇu niliyil māntak kuraṅkiliruntu māntaṉ vēṟupaṭṭavaṉ eṉṟālum māntaṉ evvaḷavu māperum koṭumiyāṉa uyiriyāka māṟi viṭṭāṉ!)
7. kaṇṭaṅkaḷ nakarvuk koḷki, maṟṟum kaṇṭaṅkaḷiṉ kaṭalōrap pakutikaḷ kaṭaluḷ mūḻkiya ceyti ākiyavaṟṟiṉ aṭippaṭiyil kaḻaka nūlkaḷiṟ kāṇum kaṭalkōḷ ceyti kaḷi iṉṟiya aṟiviyalukkēṟpa viḷakka vēṇṭiyuḷḷatu. kumari muṉikkut teṟkil nilappakutikaḷ kaṭalkōḷil mūḻkiya ceytiyik kalittoki 104m cilappatikāram kāṭu kāṇkātiyum kuṟippiṭukiṉṟaṉa. tolkāppiyattiṉ mutal urikārar ākiya (ki.pi. 10 ām nūṟṟāṇṭic cārnta) iḷampūraṇarum itik kuṟippiṭukiṟār. avarukkup piṉṉar vanta iṟiyaṉār akapporuḷ uri yāciriyarum aṭiyārkku nallārum icceytiyi mēlum virivupaṭuttic caṟṟu mikippaṭuttik kūṟukiṉṟaṉar. paṇṭit tamiḻilakkiyam kūṟum ikkaṭalkōḷ ceytiyip paṟṟi eḻutappaṭṭuḷḷa āyvurikaḷ varumāṟu: 
i) ca. cōmacuntara pārati (1913) tamiḻp paṇṭi ilakkiyaṅkaḷum tamiḻakamum, cittānta tīpikā XIV 
ii)	vi.jē. tampi piḷḷi (1913) : māṇikkavācakar toṉma varalāṟu, tamiḻiyaṉ āṇṭikuvāri II – 
iii)	 maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ (1930) : māṇikkavācakar varalāṟum kālamum 
iv) 	ē. es. vittiyanāta aiyar (1929) : "kīḻināṭukaḷiṉ
piraḷayat toṉmaṅkaḷ'' : pampāy varalāṟṟuk kaḻaka jarṉa l II -1

catapata pirāmaṇam 18 mutaliyavaṟṟil kūṟappaṭum "maṉu piraḷayam" tirāviṭat toṉmattiliruntu uruvākiyatu eṉa (iii) um (iv) um kūṟukiṉṟaṉa. cumēriyap piraḷayak katikūṭap paḻan tamiḻk kaṭalkōḷ toṉmattiliruntu uruppeṟṟatē eṉa (1) m (vi)m karutukiṉṟaṉa.
8. intiya mākkaṭalil paḻaṅkālattil oru kaṇṭam iruntu atu kaṭalil mūḻki viṭṭatu eṉṟu 19 ām nūṟṟāṇṭu iṟutiyil ēkkalum vēṟu cilarum karuti ataṟku "lemūriyā" eṉṟa peyariyum iṭṭaṉar. āṉāl iṉṟuḷḷa aṟiviya laṟiñarkaḷiṉ orumitta karuttu eṉṉa? kaṇṭam aḷavukkup periya nilapparappu etuvum entak kālattilum kaṭaluḷ mūḻkiṭa villi eṉpatē avarkaḷ muṭivu eṉak kaṇṭōm. kāntira tāv tamatu "mukkaṭaṟputirkaḷ" nūlil iti 1974lēyē cuṭṭiyuḷḷār. eṉiṉum iti yārum kaṇṭukoḷvatilli!
"toṉmi nākarikaṅkaḷip paṭitta mānta iṉaṅ kaḷik koṇṭirunta (kaṇṭam pōṉṟa ) perunilapparappukaḷ eviyum entak kālattilum intiyā, pacipik, aṭlāṇṭik peruṅkaṭalkaḷ evaṟṟilum iruntiruppataṟkāṉa vāyppu aṟavē illi eṉalām".
"It is highly improbable that big land masses, inhabited by large numbers of people who created ancient civilisations ever existed in the Indian, Pacific Or Atlantic Oceans". (p267)
ippoḻutu aṟiviyal ēṟṟuḷḷatu kaṇṭa nakarvu maṟṟum nilappāḷaṅkaḷ kōṭpāṭē (Continental drift and Plate techtonics) yākum. itaṉi viḷakkum ulakap paṭaṅkaḷ iraṇṭi aṭutta iru pakkaṅkaḷ il kāṇka. aṭutta pakkaṅkaḷil. āyiṉum ki.mu. 8,000ai oṭṭi uram (WURM) paṉiyūḻi iṟutiyil kaṭal maṭṭam uyarntu ulakeṅkum ēṟattāḻa irunūṟu, munnūṟu kal aḷavukku kaṭaṟkarip pakuti kaṭalil mūḻkiviṭṭatu eṉpati ipputiya kōṭpāṭum ēṟṟuk koḷkiṟatu. akkaṭalkōḷ kālattiṟku muṉṉar intup peruṅkaṭalil uḷḷa tīvukaḷ mūlamāka āppirikkāviyum teṉṉintiyāviyum iṇitta vāl pōṉṟa nilappakuti kaḷum cila (piṉṉar mūḻki viṭṭavi) iruntirukkalām eṉavum kūṟappaṭukiṟatu. (vālṭar pēr carvīs ; The Roots of Ancient India, 1971). ilemūriyāk kaṇṭak koḷki nilavi vanta 




kālattilum kūṭa ca. cōmacuntara pārati, mayili cīṉi vēṅkaṭacāmi, em ārōkkiyacāmi pōṉṟa ciṟanta aṟiñarkaḷ kumarikkut teṟkil kaṭaluḷ mūḻkiya pakuti kaṇṭam aḷaviṉa taṉṟu; ciṟu nilappakutiyāka irukkalām eṉṟē kuṟippiṭṭuḷḷaṉar. kā. appātturiyārum 'kaṭal koṇṭa teṉṉāṭu' eṉṟu kuṟippiṭṭati uṇarka. ippoḻutu ilemūriyāk kaṇṭak koḷki aṭippaṭiyaṟṟatu eṉṟu niṟuvappaṭṭu viṭṭatic cākkāka vittuk koṇṭu cumati irāmacāmi tamatu kaṟpaṉip puviyiyalāḷarkaḷum, pēraḻivu varalāṟu kaḷum; mūḻkiviṭṭa ilemūriyā : Fabulous geographers, catastrophic Histories : The Lost Lemuria (2004: permaṉeṉṭ piḷāk, ṭelli) eṉṉum nūlil "kaṭal koṇṭa teṉṉāṭu " paṟṟi eḻutiya tamiḻaṟiñarkaḷ aṉivariyum tākki ēḷaṉam ceytuḷḷār. 1950kaḷukkup piṉ "lemūriyāk kaṇṭak" koḷkiyi aṟiviyal aṟavē kiviṭṭu viṭṭatu eṉpatu uṇmitāṉ. āṉāl cumati irāmacāmi oṉṟik kuṟippiṭa maṟukkiṟār. atu eṉṉa? kalittoki, cilappatikāram, piṟkāla uriyāciriyar nūlkaḷ cuṭṭum kaṭal koṇṭa teṉṉāṭu ciṟiya aḷaviṉatāka iruntirukkalām eṉṟu tamiḻaka aracu 1972 il veḷiyiṭṭa tamiḻnāṭṭu varalāṟu - tol paḻaṅkālam pōṉṟa nūlkaḷ kuṟippiṭuvati avar kaṇṭu koḷḷavilli. aṉṟiya 'nāṭu' eṉpatu intiyā' tamiḻ nāṭu' pōṉṟa perunilap pakutiyaṉṟu. iṉṟiya vaṭṭa (tālukā) aḷaviṟkuḷ kūṭa iraṇṭu - mūṉṟu nāṭukaḷ uḷḷaṉavē. uriyāciriyarkaḷ kuṟitta 49 nāṭukaḷum cērntu kaṇṭam aḷavukku iruntirukka vēṇṭiyatēyilli. muṉ pattiyil kūṟiyatu pōla ki.mu. 8,000ai oṭṭik kaṇṭattiṭṭuppakuti kaṭalil mūḻkiyatiyē kaḻaka nūlkaḷil uḷḷa kaṭalkōḷ ceytikaḷ kūṟuvatākak koṇṭāl iṉṟiya aṟiviyal kaṇṭupiṭippukaḷukkuk kaḻaka (caṅka) ilakkiyak kaṭalkōḷ ceyti muṟṟilum icivatēyākum eṉpatu tēṟṟam. (kāṇka pi. irāmanātaṉ 1998, 2003 2004).
9. ivvāṟu tamiḻ, tamiḻar ceytikaḷik koccip paṭutti eḻutuvatu ivvāciriyarukku vaḻakkamāṉatē. ti intiyaṉ ekaṉāmik aṇṭ cōciyal hisṭari riviyū 38-2 ēpral - cūṉ 2001 itaḻil cumati irāmacāmi "iṉaṅkaḷiṉ eccaṅkaḷ : tolliyal (tamiḻt tēciya uṇarvu, cintuveḷi nākarikattiṉpāl īrppu" eṉṟu oru kaṭṭuri eḻutiṉār. cintu veḷi nākarikamum annākarikamuttiri eḻuttukaḷum tirāviṭam (tol tamiḻ ) cārntaviyē eṉṟu ciṟanta āyvāḷarkaḷ kamil cuvelapil (1990: tirāviṭa moḻiyiyal - ōr aṟimukam, iyal 6: tirāviṭa moḻikaḷum hārappā moḻiyum) askō parpōlā (praṇṭ liṉ ak 24,2000) karutuvati yellām ak kaṭṭuriyil cumati irāmacāmi kaṇṭu koḷḷavilli. cintuveḷi nākarikattiṉ tamiḻac cārpi valiyuṟuttum tamiḻaṟiñarkaḷi yellām (parpōlā, pārcarvīs, cuvalepil pōṉṟavarkaḷ āyvukaḷiṉ aṭippaṭiyil eḻutupavarkaḷ uṭpaṭa), avar eḷḷi nakiyāṭiyuḷḷār.
u moḻiyiṉ tōṟṟamum paravalum
10. muṉmānta iṉaṅkaḷ aṉittum pēccāṟṟal aṟṟavi, avarkaḷi ṭiyē moḻi uruvākavilli, ēṟattāḻa 1% ilaṭcam āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉāl tōṉṟiya ikkāla mānta iṉattiṭiyē (AMH) maṭṭumē ēṟattāḻa 50000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar moḻi uruvākiyatu eṉpatu iṉṟiya palatuṟi aṟiviyalāḷariṉ orumitta muṭivākum.

(inti, mitili, irācattāṉi mutaliyavaṟṟi orē inti moḻiyākak koḷvatā? taṉittaṉi moḻikaḷākak koḷvatā? itu paṟṟi ellām moḻiyiyalāḷariṭam karuttu vēṟupāṭu uṇṭu. 6000 allatu 6059 eṉpatellām oru kuttumatippēyākum.) inta 6059 moḻikaḷil 4 viḻukkāṭu ākiya 242(8+72+162) moḻikaḷip pēcuvōr eṇṇikki yāṉa 576 kōṭiyāṉatu mottam 600 kōṭi makkaḷuḷ 95 viḻukkāṭu ākum. mīti 96 viḻukkāṭu ākiya 5817 moḻikaḷip pēcuvōr veṟum 24 kōṭi makkaḷēyāvar (aṟunūṟu kōṭiyil avarkaḷ veṟum nāṉku viḻukkāṭṭiṉarēyāvar).
12. iṉṟu ulakil uḷḷa moḻikaḷ aṉittum orē mutaṉ moḻiyiliruntu tōṉṟiyirukka vāyppuṇṭu eṉṟa koḷki 19 ām nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ māksmullarukkum ici vāṉatē. 20 ām nūṟṟāṇṭil ñāla mutaṉ moḻi āyvil īṭupaṭṭa mēṉāṭṭaṟiñarkaḷ petarcaṉ, tirām petti, cuvā tecu, kirīṉperk, meriṭ rūlaṉ, illic cuvitic, tāl kōpālski, cevarōskiṉ, stārōstiṉ, pām hārṭu maṟṟum kerṉs jāṉ, peṅṭcaṉ, vāklāv pilācak pōṉṟa palarāvar. palvēṟu moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷiyum piṉvarumāṟu oru cila peruṅkuṭumpaṅkaḷukkuḷ (Super families) aṭakkalām eṉpatu avarkaḷ kaṇṭuḷḷa uṇmiyākum.
i) nāstirāṭik (intō - airōppiyaṉ, tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ, urālik, al tāyk, kārtvelliyaṉ, āprō - ēciyāṭik - atāvatu cemittiya - hāmittiyak kuṭumpam, ākiya moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷ itil aṭaṅkum). kirīṉperk vakut tuḷḷa yūrēciyāṭik peruṅkuṭumpattukkum itaṟkum atika vēṟupāṭu illi. yūrēciyāṭik - kil aṭaṅkiyavi etrus kaṉ, intō - airōppiyaṉ, urālik - yūkākīr, al tāyk, koriyaṉ - cappāṉiyam - aiṉu, kilyāk, cukōtiyaṉ, eskimō - alyūt ākiya moḻikkuṭumpaṅkaḷākum.
(2000 akṭōparil sṭāṉpōrṭu palkalikkaḻakattil pattiri rāj kiruṣṇamūrttiyi kirīṉperk cantitta poḻutu, "tirāviṭa moḻi yūrēciyāṭik peruṅkuṭumpattiṉ uṭaṉpiṟappāka irukkalām. makaḷāka irukka muṭiyātu" eṉat Og STJÓTTITII. (Bh. Krishnamurthi. The Dravidian Languages, 2003, pak. 46). itiliruntu slāv, irāṉiyaṉ, vētamoḻi camaskirutam, kirīk, ilattīṉ, keltik, cerumāṉiyam pōṉṟa moḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷuk kellām tāyāṉa intō airōppiyatti viṭat toṉmi vāyntatu tirāviṭa moḻikkuṭumpam, atāvatu paḻantamiḻ eṉṟu uṇaralām.)
ii. cīṉa - kākēciyaṉ 
iii. āstirik (muṇṭā pōṉṟavi) 
iv. amerikka intiya moḻikaḷ 
V. intō - pacipik 
vi. koycāṉ
vii. kāṅkō - cakārā 
palvēṟu moḻikkuṭumpaṅkaḷi iṇittu avaṟṟukku mūlamāṉa moḻip peruṅkuṭumpaṅkaḷik kāṇum āyvāḷar kaḷuḷ cilar ataṟkum mēlē pōy ñāla mutaṉmoḻi  (māntaṉiṉ mutal tāymoḻi) āyvukkum ceṉṟuḷḷaṉar. meriṭ rūlaṉ taṉatu moḻikaḷiṉ tōṟṟam - moḻikaḷiṉ koṭivaḻi āyvu (sṭāṉpōrṭu palkalikkaḻakam, 1994) nūliṉ pakkam 277 il kūṟuvatu varumāṟu: "perumpālāṉa moḻiyiyalaṟiñarkaḷ ottuk koḷḷāviṭiṉum allatu aiyappāṭṭuṭaṉ karutiṉum iṉṟuḷḷa moḻikaḷ aṉittum (iṉṟu iṟantuviṭṭa āṉāl muṉṉar nilaviyataṟkuc cāṉṟukaḷ kiṭṭiyuḷḷa moḻikaḷ uṭpaṭa) orē ñāla mutaṉ moḻiyi liruntu tāṉ tōṉṟiṉa eṉpataṟku valuvāṉa cāṉṟu uḷḷatu" annūliṉ pakkaṅkaḷ 277-366il 27 mutaṉmiyāṉa karuttukaḷukkup palvēṟu moḻikkuṭumpaṅ kaḷilum uḷḷa coṟkaḷ "Global Etymologies" tarappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. akkaruttukkaḷukku ñāla mutaṉ moḻiyil eṉṉa vērc col iruntirukkalām eṉṟu ūkikka atu muṉṉōṭi yākum. avaṟṟuḷ palavaṟṟukku (ēṟattāḻa 17kkut tamiḻc coṟkaḷē poruḷ potintaṉa vākavum ñālamutaṉ moḻi yiṉ vērccol vaṭivi viḷakkuvaṉa vākavum amintuḷḷaṉa.
13. irāparṭ kālṭuvel 1856il taṉatu tirāviṭa atāvatu teṉ intiya moḻikaḷiṉ oppilakkaṇam eṉ ṉum māperum nūli veḷiyiṭṭār. (tiruntiya virivāṉa iraṇṭām patippu 1875) oru puṟam tirāviṭa moḻikaḷukkum maṟupuṟam,
intō - airōppiya moḻikaḷ 
cemittiya moḻikaḷ 
elāmiṭ moḻi 
cittiya (ippoḻutu 'urāl - alṭāyk ) moḻikaḷ 
cappāṉiya moḻi
āttirēliyap paḻaṅkuṭi moḻikaḷ 
ākiyavaṟṟukkum iṭiyē kāṇum ilakkaṇa oppumikaḷ, col oppumikaḷ ākiyavaṟṟip paṟṟi ērāḷamāṉa ceyti kaḷi avar tantār. immoḻik kuṭumpaṅkaḷ pallāyiram āṇṭukaḷukku muṉ piriyumuṉṉar irunta (māntaṉ toṉ moḻiyiṉ) niliyi viḷakkuvataṟkāṉa oḷiyi ñāla mutaṉ moḻikku mika neruṅkiya tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpattic cārnta moḻikaḷiliruntutāṉ peṟṟāka vēṇṭum eṉpati acceytikaḷ valuvāka niṟuviṉa. kālṭuvel kōṭiṭṭuk kāṭṭiyati mēlum nuṇuki ārāyntu "mānta iṉa mutaṉ moḻi oṉṟākattāṉ iruntirukka vēṇṭum; mikat toṉmi vāynta toltamiḻē (tol tirāviṭam eṉa aḻikka virumpuvār avvāṟē aḻikkalām, maṟuppilli) aṉittu moḻikkuṭumpaṅkaḷukkum (intō-āriya moḻikaḷ uṭpaṭa) mūlamāṉa moḻiyākak karutappaṭa vēṇṭum" eṉṟa kōṭpāṭṭi mēlum āḻamāka virivāka niṟuviyavarkaḷ nallūr ñāṉap pirakāca aṭikaḷum ñā. tēvanēyap pāvāṇarum āvar. intō airōppiya moḻikkuṭumpa moḻikaḷiṉ (ēṉ piṟamoḻikkuṭumpa moḻikaḷukkum tāṉ) aṭippaṭic coṟkaḷ palavaṟṟukku ñāṉappirakācarum pāvāṇarum nūṟṟukkaṇakkāṉa poruttamāṉa tol - tirāviṭa vērc coṟkaḷi iṉam kāṭṭiyuḷḷaṉar. 1953il ñāṉappirakācar terivitta karuttu varumāṟu:
"intō airōppiya moḻikaḷil 'vēr kaḷ' eṉa uṉṉikkappaṭupavi poruḷ toṭarpaṟṟa veṟum kuṟiyīṭukaḷ tām. tirāviṭa moḻi vērkaḷō veṉil aiyattiṟkiṭamiṉṟip poruḷi yum kāraṇattiyum kāṭṭupavi yāka uyirōṭṭamuḷḷa muḷikkaru pōlat tōṉṟum; entak karut tōṭṭattil palvēṟu coṟkaḷ uruvāyiṉa eṉpatit teṟṟeṉa avi kāṭṭum vakiyil amintuḷḷaṉa. ittaṉmi avaṟṟukku il lātatāl intō airōppiya "vērkaḷ" iruḷ maṇṭiya poruḷaṟṟa veṟum olik kuviyalkaḷākavē tōṉṟum. avaṟṟukkum teḷivu tantu oḷi kāṭṭi uyirūṭṭam tarakkūṭiyavi tirāviṭa moḻi vērkaḷtām.''
piṟamoḻi vērkaḷukkum ikkaruttu perum aḷavukkup poruntuvatākum. ceṉṉik kiṟittuvak kallūri itaḻil (1929) ñāṉappirakācar "moḻiyiṉ tōṟṟam" eṉṟa kaṭṭuri yil "intō airōppiya moḻikaḷiṉ tantiyākiya toṉ moḻiyiṉ tōṟṟattiṟkāṉa aṭippaṭic cāṉṟukaḷ tamiḻiltāṉ kiṭṭum" eṉa muḻaṅkiyiruntār. (Tamil supplies this long looked for clue to finding the true origin of the proto Indo - European language) 
14. tirāviṭa moḻikaḷukkum piṟa moḻikaḷukkum iṭiyē yuḷḷa uṟavukaḷ kuṟittu ituvari piṉvarum āyvukaḷ nikaḻn tuḷḷaṉa:

15. tirāviṭa moḻikaḷukkum āttirēliyap paḻaṅkuṭi makkaḷiṉ moḻikaḷukkum iṭiyiluḷḷa mika neruṅkiya oppumiyi pi. irāmanātaṉ (kuppam) tirāviṭap palkalik kaḻakattiṉ tirāviṭiyaṉ sṭaṭīs 1-3; ēpral - cūṉ 2003 itaḻil veḷiyiṭṭuḷḷa kaṭṭuri virivāka niṟuvukiṟatu. tol tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar teṉ intiyāvil kaṇṭippāka 10,000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉarē iruntu irukka vēṇṭum eṉ patiyum iṅkiruntu ataṟku muṉṉarē āttirēliyap paḻaṅ kuṭi makkaḷ tol tamiḻ makkaḷiṭamiruntu pirintu ceṉṟirukka vēṇṭum eṉpatiyum akkaṭṭuri niṟuvukiṟatu.
ṅa. tol tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar paraviyatu teṟkiliruntu vaṭakku nōkkiyē yākum; tamiḻar intiyāviṉ tolkuṭikaḷ.
16. ippoḻutu pala nāṭukaḷilumuḷḷa paltuṟi aṟiñar kaḷum potuvāka ēṟṟuk kikkoṇṭuḷḷa (tavaṟāṉa) karutu kōḷiṉpaṭi tol tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar tāyakam vaṭa kiḻakku īrāṉ pakutiyākum ; aṅkiruntu ēṟattāḻa ki.mu. 3000ai oṭṭi avarkaḷ intiyā, teṉṉintiyā, ilaṅkiyi nōkki atāvatu teṟku nōkki iṭam peyarntu ceṉṟaṉar; cel lum vaḻiyil, pēruntu varum poḻutu āṅkāṅkuc cilar iṟakki viṭappaṭuvatu pōl, tirāviṭa moḻipēcum kuḻukkaḷ cila āṅkāṅku viṭṭu varappaṭṭaṉa. (kē.vi. cuvalepil (1972) : "tirāviṭarkaḷ iṟakkam " The descent of the Dravidians; tirāviṭa moḻiyiyal paṟṟiya paṉṉāṭṭu āyvitaḻ (IJDL) tokuti 2; pak. 57-63. pirittāṉiyak kalik kaḷañciyattil tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ paṟṟi avar eḻutiyuḷḷa kaṭṭuriyilum avar ik karuttiyē kūṟukiṟār. tamatu teṉṉintiya varalāṟu (4m patippu 1976) nūlil kē. ē. nīlakaṇṭa cāstiriyum itu "naṭantirukkak kūṭātataṉṟu" (not unlikely) eṉkiṟār.
17. itaṟku nērmāṟāṉa koḷki tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar vaṭakku nōkkip paraviṉar eṉṉum "tirāviṭar ēṟṟam koḷkiyākum : DRAVIDIAN ASCENT (from South). ippoḻutu atu paḻiya pāṇik koḷkiyākak karutap paṭiṉum ataṉi valiyuṟuttiyuḷḷa aṟiñarkaḷuḷ her. ār. hāl; appar īrācu (hīrās pātiriyār), pi. ti. cīnivāca aiyaṅkār, vi. ār. irāmaccantira tīṭcitar, maṟimali aṭikaḷ, yu. ār. ehareṉpels, cēviyar .es taṉināyaka aṭikaḷār, ka.ta.tirunāvukkaracu, tēvanēyappāvāṇar, piriṭjeṭ maṟṟum irēmāṇṭ ālciṉ (1988), kē.kē.piḷḷi ākiyōrum aṭaṅkuvar. cuvirā jeysvāl (1974); jē. ār.mār (1975); parṭaṉ sṭeyṉ (1998) ākiyōr tirāviṭar ēṟṟam' eṉṟa koḷkiyi ēṟkāviṭiṉum "tirāviṭar iṟakkam" eṉṟa koḷkiyiyum ēṟka maṟukkiṉṟaṉar.)
18. tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar ki.mu. 3000ai oṭṭi intiyā vukkuḷ nuḻintaṉar eṉṟa kōṭpāṭu mēlē pirivukaḷ (2) (ṅ) ākiyavaṟṟil coṉṉavaṟṟōṭu porunti varukiṟatā? moḻip peruṅ kuṭumpaṅkaḷukkiṭiyēyuḷḷa varalāṟṟuk kālattukku muntiya uṟavukaḷip paṟṟi āyvu ceyyum aṟiñar palar ittavaṟāṉa kōṭpāṭṭiṉi oṭpam iṉṟip piṉpaṟṟi varukiṉṟaṉar. eṭuttukkāṭṭāka ārpiḷeñc em.spiriks 1997 il tokuttu veḷiyiṭṭa tolliyalum moḻiyum : (1) kōṭpāṭṭu āyvuneṟik karuttōṭṭaṅkaḷ eṉṉum nūlil reṉpurū piṉvarum muṭivik kūṟukiṟār:
"(naṭuk kiḻakkup pakutikaḷil iruntu vēḷāṇmit toḻil ceyta makkaḷ paṇṭu paravat toṭaṅkiyatu paṟṟiya) tolliyal cāṉṟu kaḷiṉ aṭippaṭiyil tol intō airōp piyaṉ, tol āprō - ēciyāṭik, tol elāmiṭ, tirāviṭam, tol alṭāyk moḻi kaḷ ākiya moḻikaḷip pēciyōr aṉivarum mikac curuṅkiya miyak kiḻakku nilappakutiyil (cumēriyā, elām, iṉṟiya turukki) uṭaṉ uṟintu irukka vēṇṭum. immoḻikaḷ ellām (nāstirāṭik moḻiyiyalāḷar kūṟuvatu pōla) toṭarpuṭiyaviyāka iruppatu uṇmi yāṉāl avarkaḷellām appakutiyi viṭṭup pirintu vilakiya ki.mu. 8000 - 6000 kālakkaṭṭattukku muṉṉar oru cila āyiram āṇṭukaḷ immoḻikaḷ aṉittiṟkum mūlamāṉa nāstirāṭik moḻi an nilappakutiyil pēcappaṭṭirukka vēṇṭum eṉpatu tolliyal kaṇṭupiṭippukkaḷukkum ottu varumtarukkamākum.'' ivvāṟu tol - nāstirāṭik pēciyavarkaḷ aṉi varum 10,000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar oruṅkē naṭuk kiḻakkup pakutiyil vāḻntiruntārkaḷ eṉṟa intak kōṭpāṭṭiyum (ataṉ toṭarpāṉa "intiyāvukkuḷ aṅkiruntu tirāviṭar kaḷ iṟaṅkiyatu ēṟattāḻa ki.mu. 3000ai oṭṭittāṉ" eṉṟa kōṭpāṭṭiyum poyppippatu tirāviṭa moḻikaḷukkum āttirēliyap paḻaṅkuṭi makkaḷiṉ moḻikaḷukkum iṭiyē uḷḷa neruṅkiya piṇippu ākum. itupaṟṟi kāliṉ pi. mācikā 1999 il kūṟiyatu kuṟippiṭat takkatu.
"teṉ āciyāvil kaḻipaḻaṅkālattil oru potuvāṉa mūtāti moḻi iruntirukka vēṇ ṭum. ippōtiya mānta iṉam (ēṟattāḻa ōr ilaṭcam āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar) ulakeṅkum paravat toṭaṅkiya kāla kaṭṭamē tol tirāviṭa moḻiyiṉ toṭakkak kālam ākalām" (It may be a question of a very ancient common substratum in south Asia, Pre-Dravidian going back even to the original peopling of the world; The year Book of South Asian Languages and Linguistics, 2001 New Delhi).
19. ēṟattāḻa oṉṟari ilaṭcam āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉart ikkāla mānta iṉam āppirikkāvil tōṉṟiyatu; mutal tāymoḻi ēṟattāḻa 50000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar uruvāṉatu; iṉṟikku ōr ilaṭcam - 50,000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar iruntu teṉṉintiyā vaḻiyākavum ulakiṉ pala pakutikaḷukkum ikkāla mānta iṉam paraviyatu eṉṟum mēlē kaṇṭōm. ivvāṟu vaṭakku, vaṭakiḻakku nōkkiya mānta iṉap paravalil tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunarukku mukāmiyāṉa paṅku iruntirukka vēṇṭum. tirāviṭar ēṟṟam (Dravidian ascent) paṟṟiya intak kōṭpāṭṭi "ñāṉappirakācar - tēva nēyaṉ kōṭpāṭu" eṉa aḻikkalām. (tirāviṭa moḻi kaḷukku āttirē liyap paḻaṅkuṭi makkaḷiṉ moḻikaḷuṭaṉ uḷḷa neruṅkiya toṭarpiyum (moḻikkuṭumpaṅkaḷ piṟa vaṟṟuṭaṉ uḷḷa neṅkiya toṭarpiyum viḷakka vallatu ik kōṭpāṭēyākum. teṉṉintiyāviliruntu vaṭakkē ceṉṟa tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar uruvākkiyatē cintuveḷi nākarika mākum. (hīrās 1953; mativāṇaṉ 1995 ; irāmanātaṉ 1999; pūraṇa cantirajīvā 2004) avarkaḷukku elām, cumēriyā, ekiptu mutaliya nākarikaṅkaḷi uruvākkiyatilum paṅku iruntirukka vēṇṭum.
20. cuvelapil (1990) "ēṟattāḻa ki.mu. 10000kku muṉṉar tirāviṭam, urāl - alṭāyk, cappāṉiyam moḻi pēcunar kaḷiṭiyē neruṅkiya toṭarpu iruntirukka vēṇṭum" eṉṟu karutuvatik kaṇṭōm. attakiya toṭarpiyum "tirāviṭar ēṟṟam" eṉṉum kōṭpāṭē viḷakkavallatu. ñāṉappirakācar - tēvanēyaṉ kōṭpāṭṭiṉpaṭi ki.mu. 10,000kku muṉṉarē tol intō - airōppiyam pēcunar tol - tirāviṭam pēcu nariṭamiruntu pirintu viṭṭaṉar; naṭu āciya sṭeppi pul veḷikaḷil cila āṇṭukaḷ vāḻntaṉar; avarkaḷil cila kuḻuviṉar ki.mu. 4000-3000 aḷavil mēṟku nōkki airōppāvukkuc ceṉṟaṉar. (avarkaḷiṭam piṉṉar uruvāṉaviyē kirēkkam, ilattīṉ, kelṭik, jermāṉik, slāviya moḻik kuṭumpaṅ kaḷākum); vēṟu cila kuḻukkaḷ kiḻakku - teṉkiḻakku nōkki iṭam peyarnta kālam ki.mu. 2500ai oṭṭi ākum; avvāṟu kiḻakkē vanta kuḻuviṉar moḻikaḷil atāvatu intō āriya, irāṉiya moḻikaḷil paṇṭu (atāvatu ki.mu. 10000kku muṉṉart tol tirāviṭa moḻiyiṉariṭa miruntu pirinta kālat til) uṭaṉ koṇṭu ceṉṟa tol tirāviṭa moḻik kūṟu kaḷōṭu cērttu, vaṭamēṟku intiyāvilum appālum vāḻntu vanta tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunarkaḷiṭam iruntu (iraṇ ṭāvatu kaṭṭamāka) putitāka mēlum pala tirāviṭa moḻik kūṟukaḷ cērkkappaṭa lāyiṉa. tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar ēṟṟam (Dravidian ascent) paṟṟiya ik kōṭpāṭāṉatu tol māntar moḻiyiyal muṭivukaḷi mēlum cīrmi peṟac ceyya vallatu; nāḷaṭivil 
mānta iṉat (AMH) tōṟṟamum paravalum; 
moḻip peruṅkuṭumpaṅkaḷ uruvākkamum paravalum; 
varalāṟṟukku muntiya tolliyal 
ākiya mūṉṟu tuṟikaḷilum aṉitti yum viḷakkattakka oruṅkiṇinta oru peruṅ kōṭpāṭu (Grand synthesis) uruvākka vaḻi kōlak kūṭiyatu ākum.
21. tamatu IJDL cūṉ 2007 kaṭṭuriyil sṭepāṉ leviṭ piṉvarumāṟu "tirāviṭar ēṟṟam koḷkiyi ātarittuḷḷatu kuṟippiṭattakkatu.
"kaṭal maṭṭam ippoḻutuḷḷati viṭa mikak kuṟivāka irunta paṉiyūḻik kālattil āppirikkā - teṉṉintiyāvi iṇitta vāl pōṉṟa nila iṇippukaḷ / tīvukaḷ vaḻiyāka āp pirikkāviliruntu teṉ āciyāviṟku tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar vantirukkalāmeṉṉum kōṭ pāṭṭi pi. irāmanātaṉ valiyuṟuttukiṟār. ak kōṭpāṭṭi nāṉum ātarikkiṟēṉ. āttirēliyap paḻaṅkuṭi makkaḷip paṟṟi āyvu ceyta aṟiñar ammakkaḷiṉ moḻikaḷ tirāviṭa moḻi kaḷuṭaṉ maṭṭumē toṭarpuḷḷavi eṉak kaṇ ṭuḷḷaṉar. uṟavumuṟi (Kinship) pūmarāṅ (vaḷitaṭi ) payaṉpāṭu ākiyaviyum ammakkaḷukkum tirā viṭarukkum uḷḷa toṭarpik kāṭṭukiṉṟaṉa. ki.mu. 6000ai oṭṭi ulakeṅkum kaṭal maṭṭam uyarntu nilappakutikaḷiṉ parappu curuṅkiya poḻutu ulakiṉ piṟa pakuti makkaḷuṭiya nākarikaṅkaḷiṭam iruntu tuṇṭikkappaṭṭaṉar āstirēliyap paḻaṅkuṭi makkaḷ. avarkaḷ teṉṉintiyāviliruntu āstirēliyāvukkup pulam peyarntatu ēṟattāḻa 40,000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉar irukkalām eṉkiṉṟa ṉar āstirēliya aṟiñar. itiliruntu kuṟintatu 40,000 āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉarē tirāviṭar intiyā vil iruntaṉar eṉpatu teḷivākiṟatu. inta ('tirāviṭar ēṟṟam") kōṭpāṭṭiṉpaṭi tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar teṉṉintiyāviliruntu vaṭanāṭu celkiṉṟaṉar; piṉṉar aṅkiruntu pāracīkat tiṟkum atit tāṇṭip piṟa nilappakutikaḷukkum celkiṉṟaṉar. antap piṟa nilappakutikaḷil mūla tirāviṭa moḻik kuṭumpattiliruntu pirintu urā lik, al tāyik, intō - airōppiyam ākiya moḻikkuṭumpaṅkaḷ uruvākiṉṟaṉa. intō - airōppiya moḻik kuṭumpattukku tirāviṭam tāyā? tamakkiyā? eṉpati iṉṟiya niliyil tiṭṭavaṭṭamākak kūṟa iyalātu. oppaṉ himar (2003) The Real Eve : Modern Man Journey out of Africa nūliṉpaṭi marapaṇu āyvum (kākacas iṉattic cuṭṭum M 17) tol tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar intiyāviliruntu vaṭakku vaṭamēṟkāka ēṟattāḻa 40000 āṇṭukku muṉṉar miya kiḻakku - miya āciyā - airōppā eṉṟavāṟu paṭip paṭiyākap paraviyirukkalām eṉṟa kōṭpāṭṭiṟku etirāka illi eṉpatik kāṇalām (ṭākṭar leviṭ - takaval). 
ca. tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar paṇṭu teṟkiliruntu vaṭakkākap paraviya poḻutu (Dravidian Ascent) kuṭiyēṟiya pakutiyil uruvākiya cintuveḷi nākarikam.
22. cintuveḷi nākarikam 1947kku muntiya intiyā, pākistāṉ, vaṅkāḷa tēcam cērnta intiyāviṉ parappaḷavil kāl pakutikku mēl parappaḷavu koṇṭa peru nilap pakutiyil vaṭamēṟku intiyāvil paravi iruntatu. atāvatu cumār 5 laṭcam caturamil parappaḷavil vaḻaṅkiyatu. paṇṭiya mecapotāmiya, ekiptiya nākarikaṅkaḷiṉ parappiviṭa atika māṉa parappil cintuveḷi nākarikam vaḻaṅkiyatu. an nākarikam vaḻaṅkiya pakutiyil ki.mu. 2000 vākkil mottam aintiliruntu pattu laṭcam makkaḷ vāḻntirukkalām eṉpatu posel, par pōlā pōṉṟavarkaḷ karuttu ākum (appoḻutu ulakil irunta motta makkaḷ tokiyē 9 kōṭikkum kuṟivu). cintu veḷi nākarikac ciṉṉaṅkaḷ 1500kkum mēṟpaṭṭa iṭaṅ kaḷil uḷḷaṉa. avaṟṟil cumār 20 iṭaṅkaḷil maṭṭumē - mokeñcōtarō, harappā uṭpaṭa - akaḻvāyvukaḷ naṭan tuḷḷaṉa. cintuveḷi nākarikat toṭakkam ki.mu. 7000 liruntu eṉpati meharkār akaḻvāyvu niṟuviyuḷḷatu. an nāka rikattiṉ ciṟappuṟṟa nili ki.mu. 3200 - 1800 kāla aḷavic cārntatu. mokañcōtarō nakara makkaḷ toki 40,000 eṉṟum harappā nakara makkaḷ toki 25000 eṉṟum matip piṭappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa.
23. māntar iṉa (A MH) paraval, moḻiyiṉ tōṟṟamum paravalum ākiyavaṟṟiṉ aṭippaṭiyil tirāviṭa moḻi pēcunar paṇṭu teṟkiliruntu vaṭakkākap paraviya poḻutu (Dravidian Ascent) kuṭiyēṟiya pakutiyē cintuveḷi nākarikap pakuti eṉṟu koḷvataṟkāṉa ātāraṅkaḷi ituvari kaṇ ṭōm. annākarikam tirāviṭac cārpuṭiyatu eṉpataṟku uḷḷa mēlum pala valuvāṉa ātāraṅkaḷil cila varumāṟu:
1) mokañcōtārō - harappā iṭipāṭukaḷil tirāviṭak kaṭṭumāṉak kaliyik kāṇalām. vīṭukaḷil payaṉpaṭṭa poruḷkaḷ, naki (āparaṇaṅkaḷ, parutti āṭikaḷ aṉittum tirāviṭac cārpuṭiyavi. 
ii) tamiḻariṉ ciṟṟilakka muṟiyiyē cintuveḷi nākarika eṭi aḷavukaḷil kāṇkiṟōm eṉpatic ceṅkam ku. vēṅkaṭācalam niṟuviyuḷḷār.
iii) iṉṟiya intu matattiṉ mutaṉmik kūṟukaḷ aṉit tum tirāviṭa - tamiḻ - cintuveḷi nākarikak kūṟukaḷē ākum. camaṇa , - putta mataṅkaḷiṉ tōṟṟattukkum cintuveḷi maṟṟum vaṭanāṭṭil paṇṭu irunta tamiḻ (tirāviṭa) ñāṉikaḷē kāraṇamāvar. ikkarutti ṭilar (1973) piṉvarumāṟu kūṟuvār;
iṉṟiya vēta matak karuttukkaḷ - avaṟṟiṟku etirāṉa camaṇa puttam mutaliya karuttukkaḷ ivaṟ ṟiṟku iṭiyē mutalil uṟaḻvu, piṉṉar iṇippu eṉṟavāṟu intumatam uruvāṉatu eṉṟu karutuvatu avvaḷavu cariyāṉatalla. cintuveḷi nākarikam cārnta, paṇṭiya tollintiya (tamiḻiya) nāka rikattiṉ puttuyirppē, maṟumalarcciyē iṉṟiya intumatam ākum.
(The Hindu synthesis was less the dialectical reduction of orthodoxy and heterodoxy than the
resurgence of the ancient aboriginal Indus civilisation) maṟimaliyaṭikaḷum, cīṉivāca aiyaṅkārum (1903,1923,1930) ikkaruttiṉarēyāvar.
iv) iṉṟiya icikkali āriyar tamiḻariṭamiruntu kaṟṟatē (ō. kōsvāmi "intiya ici varalāṟu, 1957)
24. cintuveḷi akaḻvāyvukaḷukku neṭuṅkālam muṉṉarē heviṭ (1888). maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ (1903) pōṉṟa nuṇmāṇuḻi pulamikka aṟiñar āriyar varukikku muṉṉar intiyā muḻuvatum vāḻntu vanta nākarika mikka tamiḻiya makkaḷiṭamiruntē āriyar nākarikam peṟṟirukka vēṇṭum eṉṟa karuttit teḷivākak kūṟiyiruntaṉar. ivaṟṟiṉ virivi pi. irāmanātaṉ : cintu veḷit tol tamiḻ nākarikam 1999 iyalkaḷ 3-6 maṟṟum 8 il kāṇka).
25. speyiṉ nāṭṭic cārnta tirāviṭar eṉṟu taṉṉi aḻittuk koṇṭa es. hīrās pātiriyār (1191888 - 14.12. 1955) toṉmi intō - naṇṇilakkari nākarika āyvu (Studies in Proto - Indo Mediterranean Culture 1953) eṉṉum nūlil cintu veḷi nākarikam tirāviṭaruṭiyatu; avarkaḷ moḻi tirāviṭa moḻi eṉpati niṟuviyuḷḷār. mikap paḻaṅ kālattil (ki.mu. 5000kkum neṭuṅkālattukku muṉṉar) mutaṟkaṇ kāvirik kariyil uruvākiya arappā nākarikam intiya mēṟkukkari vaḻiyākat tamiḻarkaḷāl cintuveḷi, cumēriyā, ekiptu naṇṇilakkari nāṭukaḷ vari koṇṭu cellappaṭṭatu eṉpatu hīrās koḷki (avar vātat tukku ātāramākak koṇṭa ceytikaḷ cila ippoḻutu tavaṟākat tōṉṟalām; cintuveḷi muttirikaḷi avar tirāviṭa moḻi cārntavi eṉṟu koṇṭa muṭivu cari yeṉṟālum avar muttirikaḷil paṭittuk kaṇṭa vācakaṅkaḷ iṉṟu ēṟkattakkaṉavāka illi. iruntālum avar kaṇṭa aṭippaṭi uṇmi iṉṟum valuvuṭiyatākavē uḷḷatu.)
26. eṉ lākōvari 1963il veḷiyiṭṭa tirāviṭar tōṟṟamum mēl nāṭukaḷum (Dravidian Origins and the West) eṉṉum nūl ciṟanta moḻiyiyal meymmikaḷi uṇarttu vatākum. 'tirāviṭar naṇṇilakkari nāṭukaḷiliruntu pulam peyarntu vantu intiyāvil cintuveḷiyil mutaṟkaṇ kuṭiyēṟip piṉṉar kumari vari paraviṉar" eṉṟa apattakkoḷ kiyi avar (vivaram puriyāmal) piṉpaṟṟiyirunta pōtilum avar niṟuviya piṉvarum meymmikaḷ mukkiyamāṉavi :
i) 6000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar intiyāviliruntu aṭlāṇṭik kaṭalvari orē mātiriyāṉa "pala col piṇippu oṭṭunili' (Polysynthetic Suffixal) moḻikaḷ iṭiyīṭu iṉṟip paraviyiruntaṉa. avaṟṟiṉ coṟ kaḷañciyaṅkaḷ ōraḷavukku oṉṟupōl iruntaṉa. potuc col viḻukkāṭu moḻikku moḻi māṟupaṭṭu iruntirukkalām. tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ, elāmiṭ, cumēriyaṉ, harri, kākēciyaṉ, halṭi , pōṉṟavi orē moḻiyamippuk koṇṭu oṉṟukkoṉṟu uṟavuṭiyaviyākac caṅkilit toṭarpōla amintiruntaṉa.
ii) intō airōppiya moḻi pēcuvōr (ki.mu 2000ai oṭṭi airōppāvukkuḷ kiḻakkiliruntu nuḻiyumuṉṉar aṅku pēcappaṭṭu vanta moḻikaḷil oṉṟāṉa pāsku (yūskarā) iṉṟum pirāṉcukkum speyiṉukkum iṭippaṭṭa pireṉīs malippakutiyil pēcappaṭṭu varukiṟatu. tol tirāviṭa moḻi inta pāsku moḻiyuṭaṉ maṭṭumaṉṟi intō airōppiya moḻikaḷukku muṉṉar airōppāvil pēcappaṭṭa toṉmoḻikaḷuṭaṉum uṟavuṭiyatu.
iii) immoḻikaḷ aṉittum oṉṟukkoṉṟu neruṅ kiya toṭarpuḷḷavi; avi potuvāṉa orē tāy moḻi yiṉ vaṭṭāra vaḻakkukaḷ eṉṟē kūṟalām.
27. cintuveḷi nākarikam intō āriya moḻi pēcunar uruvākkiyatāka irukkalām eṉa iṉṟiya intiya aṟiñar (kuṟippāka vaṭanāṭṭaṟiñar) cilar kūṟukiṉṟaṉar. itu tavaṟu eṉpatic caṟṟē viḷakkuvōm. vēta moḻiyākiya intō airōppiya moḻi intiyāvil uruvākiyirukkalām eṉṟu vēta camaskirutappaṟṟāḷar kūṟuvatu apattam (itu vēṟu; ki.mu. 10000kku muṉṉar tol tirāviṭa moḻiyiliruntu kiḷitta moḻikkuṭumpaṅkaḷuḷ intō airōppiya moḻik kuṭumpamum oṉṟu eṉṟa ñāṉappirakācar - tēvanēyaṉ kōṭpāṭu vēṟu!) ki.mu. 1700 -1500 kālakaṭṭattil intiyā viṟkuḷ vaṭamēṟkiliruntu nuḻintavarkaḷ vētamoḻi (Vedic Language) ākiya intō āriya moḻi pēcunar āvar. ivar kaḷ cintuveḷi nākarika makkaḷōṭu oppiṭum pōtu ciṟu eṇṇikkiyiṉarākavē iruntirukka vēṇṭum. intō airōppiya moḻip peruṅkuṭumpattil aṭaṅkiyavi intō āriya moḻiyākiya vētamoḻi tavira irāṉiyaṉ (avestaṉ), aṉaṭōliyaṉ (hiṭṭiṭ), armīṉiyaṉ, tōkkāriyaṉ, alpēṉiyaṉ, kirīkku , ittālik (ilattīṉ mutaliyavi), kelṭik (airiṣ, velṣ uṭpaṭa), jermāṉik (āṅkilam uṭpaṭa), pālṭik (laṭviyaṉ, littuvēṉiyaṉ), slāv (raṣyaṉ mutaliyavi) ākiyaviyākum. intap pal vēṟu intō airōppiya moḻi pēcunarum oruṅku cērntu ki.mu. 4000 - 3000 kāla aḷavil karuṅkaṭal - kāspiyaṉ kaṭal pakutikku vaṭakkil mattiya āciya - airōppiya sṭeppi pulveḷikaḷil vacittu vanta nāṭōṭikaḷ (nomads) āvar. ak kālakkaṭṭattil avarkaḷ intō airōppiyat toṉ moḻiyip pēcivantaṉar. ki.mu. 3000 - 2000 kāla aḷavil teṉ kiḻakkākavum nakaralāyiṉar. mēṟkil ceṉṟa nāṭōṭik kuḻuviṉaruḷ oṉṟāṉa kirīkku moḻi pēcunar nuḻinta pakuti yil (tirāviṭac cārpāṉa) appakutip paḻaṅkuṭi makkaḷuṭaṉ kalantu kirīkku moḻi pēcunar uṭaṉaṭiyāka nākarikam peṟṟaṉar. kiḻakku teṉkiḻakkākap peyarnta nāṭōṭik kuḻuviṉaruḷ intō āriya moḻi pēcum kuḻuvum oṉṟu. ki.mu. 1700 - 1500il avarkaḷ intiyāvukkuḷ irāṉiliruntu nuḻinta poḻutu avarkaḷum cintuveḷit tol nākarikattiṉariṭam miruntu virivil nākarikam peṟṟaṉar. cintuveḷit tirāviṭa aṟiñarkaḷ (avarkaḷil cilar riṣikaḷākavum ēṟkappaṭṭaṉar) paṭippukaḷum nēraṭiyākavō moḻi peyarkkappaṭṭō rik vētattilēyē ēṟiyuḷḷaṉa eṉpatu maṟimaliyaṭikaḷ (1903, 1923, 1930), vālparṭ pōṉṟōr karuttākum.
taṅka ḷuṭiya The Rise of Civilisation in India and Pakistan (1988nūlil piriṭjaṭ maṟṟum rēmāṇṭ ālciṉ tampatiyar. karuttum ituvē :
(The earliest Indo-Aryan text, the compiled Rg Veda, shoWS several influences of a non-Indo-Aryan, Dravidian element, in the form of phonetic changes, introduction of loan words and names etc.These presuppose the coexistence of the Vedic and Dravidian speaking peoples in a cultural contact situation for a period, perhaps of centuries, before the compilation of the Rg Veda (circa 15001300BC)
28. mutaṟkaṇ intō āriya moḻi pēcunarukkum cintu veḷi nākarikat tamiḻarukkum (tirāviṭarukkum iṭiyē vaṉ muṟi ēṟpaṭṭa pōtilum virivil iruvakiyārum kalantu viṭṭaṉar. āriyarāl nakaraṅkaḷ tākkappaṭṭirukkalām. intira ṉiṉ peyarē purantaraṉ (nakaraṅkaḷi aḻippavaṉ) tāṉē nakaraṅ kaḷiṉ vīḻccikku ki.mu. 1700ai oṭṭi nikaḻnta iyaṟki, cuṟṟup puṟac cūḻnilik kāraṇaṅkaḷum ōraḷavu kāraṇamāyirun tirukkalām eṉṟu ālciṉ (1995) kūṟukiṟār. āṉāl cintu veḷi nākarikam paraviyirunta ciṟṟūrppuṟap perunilap parappil (ciṟu eṇṇikkiyil nuḻinta) āriyarāl peruntāk kam ēṟpaṭuttiyirukka muṭiyātu. ākavētāṉ cunītikumār cāṭṭarji, vālṭar pēr carvīs, es.ē. ṭilar pōṉṟōr kūṟuvatu pōla iṉṟiya nākarikam, paṇpāṭu, intumatam ākiyavaṟṟiṉ aṭittaḷam (rūpāykku 12 aṇā aḷavukku eṉpār caṭṭarji) tirāviṭa (tamiḻ) moḻi, paṇpāṭu āki yaviyē. intō āriya moḻi pēcunar paraval paṟṟiya varalāṟṟiliruntē avarkaḷ ki.mu. 1700kku muntiya cintu nākarikatti uruvākkiyirukka iyalātu eṉpatu teriyum. cintu veḷi nākarikac ciṉṉaṅkaḷil āriyarukku nerukkamāṉa kutiri kiṭiyātu eṉpatum mukkiyamāṉatu.
29. intō āriya moḻiyiṉ rikvēta niliyilēyē nuḻintuḷḷa tirāviṭac coṟkaḷāka cumār nāṟpatu - aimpatu coṟkaḷi parō, emaṉō pōṉṟavarkaḷ ēṟkeṉavē aṭiyāḷam kaṇṭuḷḷaṉar. tirāviṭa moḻiyiyal - ōr aṟi mukam (1990) nūlil itik kuṟippiṭum cuvelapil, rik vētak kālattiliruntē intō āriya moḻi kaṭaṉ peṟṟuḷḷa ēṟattāḻa 10 moḻiyiyaṟ kūṟukaḷiyum paṭṭiyaliṭṭuḷḷār. (iviyellām vēta camaṟkiruta moḻiyil tirāviṭa moḻi yiṉ tākkam eṉṟa aḷaviltāṉ ivarkaḷ āyvukaḷ uḷḷaṉa. āṉāl ñāṉap pirakācarum pāvāṇarum kaṇṭa muṭivu kaḷ ivaṟṟiviṭa mika viñciyavi. avviruvarum "intō - airōppiya moḻikaḷiṉ aṭippaṭi vērc coṟkaḷ palavum tol - tirāviṭa moḻiyiliruntu peṟṟaviyē" eṉṟa karuttuṭiyavar.
30. cintuveḷi nākarikam tirāviṭa nākarikam eṉṟum anta nākarika muttiri eḻuttukkaḷ tol tamiḻē eṉṟum takuti vāynta aṟiñar aṉivarum ēṟkiṉṟaṉar. tamatu Indus Script Dravidian (1995) nūlil irā. mativāṇaṉ, cintu veḷiyil muntu tamiḻ (2004) nūlil pūrṇa cantira jīvā ākiyōr av veḻuttukkaḷit toltamiḻākap paṭikkum poḻutu tol kāppiyattiliruntu kiṭṭum moḻiyiyal taravukaḷiyum payaṉpaṭuttiyuḷḷaṉar. puṟanāṉūṟu 201 202 il kuṟippiṭappaṭum iruṅkōvēḷiṉ muṉṉōr cintuveḷi nākarikattōṭu toṭarpuṭiyavarkaḷākak kapilarāl karutappaṭṭaṉar. puṟanāṉūṟu 202 kuṟippiṭum ariyam rikvētam 6.27.5 il cuṭṭappaṭum hariyūpiyā tāṉ eṉpatu pi.el. cāmi karuttu (centamiḻc celvi caṉavari 1994). ariyam = aracamaram. ariya + akappā = ariyakappā = harappā eṉṟu peyar māṟiyatu eṉkiṟār avar. iruṅkōvēḷiṉ muṉṉōr 49 talimuṟikaḷukku muṉṉar (atāvatu tol paḻaṅkālattil) pukaḻpeṟṟa tuvari eṉṉum kōṭṭi nakari āṇṭu vantaṉar eṉṟu puṟam 201 kūṟukiṟatu. tamatu tolkāppiyap pāyira vuriyil nacciṉārkkiṉiyar vēḷir tuvārapati (tuvārakiyil liruntu vantavarkaḷ eṉkiṉṟār. ariyam harappāvit tāṉ kuṟippatākak koṇṭāl harappā paṟṟiyum tuvāraki paṟṟiyum kapilar kālattil vaḻaṅkiya (oru nakarukkuriyati maṟṟataṟkuriyatāka māṟṟi vaḻaṅkiya) toṉmak karuttip puṟam 201,202 pāṭalkaḷ kūṟukiṉṟaṉa eṉka. itaṉi jīvā mēlum āyvu ceykiṟār. vēḷ - vēṭ - pēṭ (Bet) - pēṭ tuvārakā eṉṟu tuvārakiyiṉ peyar varalāṟṟi avar tarukiṟār.
31. cumēriya nākarika muttirikaḷil ciṅkaṅkaḷ iraṇṭik kollum kilkāmeṣ uruvam kāṇappaṭukiṟatu. kilkāmēṣ paṟṟiya toṉmak katiyum cumēriyap poṟippukaḷil tarap paṭukiṟatu. cintuveḷi muttirikaḷ iraṇṭil (kilkāmēṣ ciṅkaṅkaḷit tākkuvatu pōlavē) intiya vīraṉ oruvaṉ pulikaḷ iraṇṭi kikku oṉṟākak kolvatu pōl cit tirikkappaṭṭuḷḷāṉ. puṟam 201 iruṅkōvēḷip 'puli kaṭimāl' eṉṟu aḻikkiṟatu. iruṅkōvēḷiṉ muṉṉōr kālam cintuveḷi nākarikak kālattiliruntu toṭaṅki yirukkalām eṉa ai.makātēvaṉ Journal of Tamil Studies mē 1970 itaḻil uṉṉittiruntār. āyiṉum 2002 caṉavariyil avar karuttu anta uṉṉippiṟku valuvāṉa ātāram illi eṉpatākum.
32. rikvētam ai . 133,1 & 3iṉ tamiḻ vaṭivam varumāṟu; 
"ō makavāṉ aḻintupaṭṭa vilastāṉaka nakarattilum, aḻintupaṭṭa makāvilasta nakarattilum uḷḷa cūṉiyak kārikaḷ kumpalkaḷi aḻittu oḻippāyāka" 
"nāṉ mēlulakattiyum pūmiyiyum cattiyattiṉāl tūymip paṭuttukiṟēṉ. vilastāṉa nakaril intiraṉāl tōṟkaṭikkap paṭṭu koliyuṇṭu kiṭakkum intiraṉi eritta āṟṟal mikka tuṣṭap picācukaḷi nāṉ erittu oḻikkiṟēṉ" 
āriyarkaḷāl nācamākkappaṭṭa nakarattiṉ peyarāṉa 'vilas tāṉam' eṉpatu āriya moḻiyallāta piṟamoḻic collāka irukka vēṇṭum eṉpatu ṭākṭar parō karuttu ākum. (jarṉal āp intiyaṉ hisṭari XII -1 ēpral 1963) orukkāl 'vilastāṉam' eṉpatu vēḷiruṭiya ūrnakarik kuṟittirukkalām.
33. cintu mutaliya āṟukaḷil cintuveḷit tirāviṭarkaḷ kaṭṭiyirunta aṇikaḷi āriyar uṭittu nāṭṭi veḷḷak kāṭākki aḻittirukkalām eṉpar tāmōtar tarmāṉant kōcampi (The culture and Civilisation of Ancient India in historical outline 1974). maturik kāñci 725 ām aṭiyilum aka nāṉūṟu 346 ām pāṭalilum āṟṟiṉ kuṟukkē kaṭṭappaṭum aṇikaḷik kuṟikka 'kaṟciṟi' eṉṉum col payaṉpaṭukiṟatu. rik vētattil aṇiyik kuṟippiṭum cirā (Sira) eṉṉum col 'kaṟciṟiyiṉ' citivē eṉpar pi.el. cāmi (centamiḻc celvi : 1994 navampar)
ru. cintuveḷi nākarika muttiri eḻuttukkaḷ tamiḻiya moḻiyākap paṭikkattakkaviyē eṉpatum tamiḻ vari vaṭivam cintu veḷi varivaṭivattiliruntē uruvāki yirukkalām eṉpatum
34. cintuveḷi nākarika muttiri eḻuttukkaḷ eṉpavi mūvāyirattukku mēṟpaṭṭa "sṭīyaṭṭiṭ" (cavarkkārakkal, atāvatu mākkal ) muttiri kaḷilum cila cepput takaṭukaḷ pōṉṟavaṟṟilum uḷḷavi. ovvoru muttiriyum cumār 20 mi.mī. x 30 mi.mī. aḷavuḷḷatu. cila caturamāṉavi. perum pālaṉavaṟṟil oru vilaṅkiṉ uruvamum ataṉ mēl pakkattil oṉṟiliruntu pattu paṉṉiraṇṭu (carācari 5) kuṟiyīṭukaḷum uḷḷaṉa. mika nīṇṭa toṭar 26 kuṟiyīṭukaḷ koṇṭatu. cintu veḷiyiliruntu paruttit tuṇi pōṉṟavaṟṟi cumēriyā pōṉṟa mēli nāṭukaḷukku ēṟṟumati ceyyumpoḻutu cippaṅkaḷ kaṭṭi avaṟṟiṉ mēl poruḷukkuriyavar peyarik kaḷimaṇṇil muttiriyiṭa im muttirikaḷuḷ perum pālāṉavi payaṉpaṭṭirukkalām eṉpatē ciṟanta āyvaṟiñar kaḷ karuttu. vīlar, haṇṭa r, kāṭ (Gadd), kōcampi, kō (Coe) ākiyōr inta aṭippaṭiyiltāṉ muttirik kuṟiyīṭukaḷil uḷḷavi taṉi āṭkaḷiṉ peyarkaḷākattāṉ (cila nērvu kaḷil paṭṭaṅkaḷuṭaṉ) irukka vēṇṭum eṉṟa muṭivukku vantuḷḷaṉar. cippattik kaṭṭiya kayiṟu, pāy ivaṟṟiṉ cuvaṭukaḷ cila kaḷimaṇ kaṭṭikaḷil kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. (cila kaṭṭikaḷ orōvaḻi neruppiṉ vāyppaṭṭu cuṭappaṭṭatāl avi maṭṭumē iṉṟu kiṭikkiṉṟaṉa. avaṟṟiltāṉ iv vaṭiyāḷaṅkaḷ terikiṉṟaṉa) āka, ālciṉ (1998) kūṟuvatu pōl "im muttirikaḷiṉ payaṉpāṭu (allatu payaṉpāṭu kaḷil oṉṟu) vāṇika naṭavaṭikkikaḷ cārntatāka iruntirukka vēṇṭum eṉpatil aiyamilli " (There can be little doubt that the Harappan seals were - atleast as one of their functions - necessary elements in the mechanism of trade. p. 185.) cila muttirikaḷ tāyat tukkaḷākavum nērttik kaṭaṉ villikaḷākavum aṭiyāḷa ilacciṉikaḷākavum payaṉpaṭuttappaṭṭu irukkalām.
35. pala muttirikaḷil oṟṟik kompu teriyum erutu oṉṟiṉ muṉṉar eṉṟa vaṭivam uḷḷatu. makātēvaṉ karuttu (1985, 1984) ivvaṭivam cōmaccāṟu vaṭikkappaṭṭa ēṉam; piṉṉar cōmaccāṟu āriyarkaḷālum mukkiyamāṉatāka ēṟkappaṭṭatu eṉpatākum. piṟkālattil ivvaṭivam intirat vajam āṉatu. aḷakkuṭi cīttārāmaṉ karūril kaṇṭeṭutta ceppu muttiriyilum inta vaṭivam uḷḷatu eṉpatu makā tēvaṉ karuttu ākum.
36. cintuveḷi eḻuttuk kuṟiyīṭukaḷ ēṟattāḻa 400 aḷavil uḷḷaṉa. (parpōlā 385, makātēvaṉ 417). 2906 muttiri vācakaṅkaḷil kaṇṭa kuṟiyīṭukaḷi makātēvaṉ piṉvarumāṟu vakippaṭuttukiṟār:

80 viḻukkāṭu taṭavikku mēl varum kuṟiyīṭukaḷ 67 maṭṭumē yākum. (pātikku mēṟpaṭṭa kuṟiyīṭukaḷ ovvoṉṟum pattu taṭavikkum kuṟivā kavē varukiṉṟaṉa. avaṟṟuḷḷum 112 ovvoṉṟum oru taṭaviyē varukiṟatu). āka, inta 67 kuṟiyīṭukaḷukkum mutaṉmi tantu vācikka muṟpaṭuvatu nallatu eṉkiṟār makātēvaṉ. mottam cumār 400 kuṟiyīṭukaḷil aṭippaṭik kuṟiyīṭukaḷ 200 eṉpatu parpōlā karuttu.
37. cintuveḷi muttiri eḻuttukkaḷ paḻantamiḻē (paḻan tirāviṭamē) eṉpati ittuṟi vallunar aṉivarum ēṟ kiṉṟaṉar. ivaṟṟip paṭittuḷḷa aṟiñarkaḷ (parpōlā, makā tēvaṉ, mativāṇaṉ, pūrṇa cantira jīvā , pēr carvīs mutali yōr) ovvoruvarum ovvoru vakiyāka vācit tuḷḷaṉar. muttiriyik kuttiyapiṉ kiṭikkum muttirip pativil uḷḷa vācakaṅkaḷi (Impressions) valamiruntu iṭamākavē perumpālōr paṭikkiṉṟaṉar. (mativāṇaṉ maṭṭum iṭamiruntu valamākap paṭittuḷḷār. eṉiṉum im muttiri eḻuttukkaḷ, tirāviṭa moḻiyic cārntatākat tāṉ irukka vēṇṭum eṉpatu paṟṟi maṭṭum aṟiñarkaḷiṭam orumitta karuttu uḷḷatu; piṉvaruvaṉavaṟṟik kāṇka :
sṭāṉli vārparṭ : intiyāvukku ōr aṟimukam eṉṟa nūlil uḷḷa "pala vakiyāṉa ātāraṅkaḷiliruntum nām karutuvatu avarkaḷ tol tirāviṭarkaḷ eṉpatum, tamiḻukkup pāṭṭaṉ muṟiyākak kūṭiya oru moḻi avarkaḷ moḻiyāka iruntirukkalām eṉpatum ākum."
“We assume from various shreds of evidence that they are were proto Dravidian, possibly using a language that was a grandfather of modern Tamil” (Stanley Wolpert: An Introduction to India, University of California Press 1991)
jē.em. rāparṭs : peṅkuyiṉ ulaka varalāṟu "teṉ ṉintiyāvil iṉṟum vaḻaṅkivarum tirāviṭa moḻikaḷōṭu iyipuṭiya oru moḻiyic cārntaviyāka (cintuveḷi muttiric coṟkaḷ) irukkalāmeṉat tōṉṟukiṟatu."
It now seems at least likely that they are part of a language akin to the Dravidian tongues still used in southern India (J.M. Roberts History of the World Pelican 1992).
kamil cuvelapil : tirāviṭa moḻiyiyal - ōr aṟimukam ''cintu veḷi eḻuttukkaḷiṉ moḻi eṉṉa eṉṟu iṟutiyāka niṟuvappaṭum poḻutu atu tirāviṭa moḻi cārntatāka amivataṟkē vāyppu mika atikam.''
The most probable candidate is and remins some form of Dravidian” (Dravidian Liguistics - An Introduction. Pondicherry 1990; Chap VI : Dravidian and Harapan)
38. cintu veḷi muttiri eḻuttukkaḷiliruntē caṅka kālat tamiḻ eḻuttukkaḷ uruvāyiṉa eṉpatu mativāṇaṉ karuttu. ēṟattāḻa ki.mu. 700kku muṉṉamē tamiḻukku ōr eḻuttu vari vaṭivam iruntatu; atip piṉpaṟṟiyē acōka pirāmi eḻuttu uruvākkappaṭṭatu eṉak koḷvataṟkāṉa ātāraṅkaḷ uṇṭu. 2006 pipravari mātattil mayilāṭutuṟi - cem piyaṉ kaṇṭiyūril vi. caṇmukanātaṉ kaṇṭeṭutta putuk kaṟkālak karuvi oṉṟil cintuveḷi eḻuttukkaḷ nāṉku uḷḷaṉa. avaṟṟi ai. makātēvaṉ "muruku aṉ" (murukaṉ) eṉṟu paṭittuḷḷār. akkaruviyiṉ kālam ki.mu. 2000 -1500 āka irukkalām eṉṟu avar karutukiṟār. putuk kaṟkālat tamiḻ makkaḷum akkālattil vāḻnta cintuveḷi nākarika makkaḷum  orē moḻiyiyē pēciṉār eṉṟum ammoḻi tirāviṭa moḻiyē eṉṟum ik kaṇṭupiṭippu niṟuvukiṟatu eṉpatum avar karuttu . ("The Neolithic people of Tamilnadu and the Indus Valley people shared the same language which can only be Dravidian and not Indo-Aryan"); 152006. intu nāḷitaḻ.
cu. varalāṟṟuk kālattukku caṅka kālattukku) muntiya (Proto Historic) kālat tamiḻaka varalāṟu ki.mu. 1000 - ki.mu. 300) 
39. tamiḻaka varalāṟṟil varalāṟṟuk kālat toṭakkam eṉa potuvāka iṉṟu ēṟkappaṭum caṅka kālam kimu. 300 - ki.pi. 300 cārntatu. ataṟku muntiya varalāṟṟukku muntiya kālam" ākiya ki.mu. 1000 - 300 paṟṟiya ceytikaḷit tiṟampaṭat taruvatu rājaṉ (2004) tolliyal nōkkil caṅkakālam ākum. āticcanallūril uḷḷa ki.mu. 1000 kālattiya iṭukāṭu 114 ēkkar parappuḷḷatu. atil 1 viḻukkāṭu maṭṭumē akaḻvāyvu ceyyappaṭṭuḷḷatu. koṭumaṇalil (caṅkakālak koṭu maṇam) 50 ēkkar parappuḷḷa paḻiya vāḻviṭattilum 1 viḻukkāṭu maṭṭumē akaḻvāyvu ceyyappaṭṭuḷḷatu. inta oru viḻukkāṭu akaḻvāyvukaḷil kiṭitta eccaṅkaḷiliruntē kimu 1000 mutal tamiḻakattil vāḻnta makkaḷē, iṭiyīṭiṉṟi, caṅka ilakkiyam vivarikkum tamiḻar camutāya makkaḷāyiṉar eṉṟu niṟuva iyaṉṟuḷḷa tu. makātēvaṉ (2003) Early Tamil Epigraphy nūlil tamiḻik kalveṭṭukaḷ evaṟṟiyum ki.mu. 200kku muntiyatākak karutavilli. āṉāl avaṟṟi (kuṟippāka koṭumaṇal pāṉi ōṭṭut tamiḻip poṟippukaḷi) ki.mu 4 allatu 5 nūṟṟāṇṭiṉavākat tāṉ koḷḷa vēṇṭum . eṉpataṟkāṉa ātāraṅkaḷi rājaṉ tantuḷḷār. mēlē pakuti āṟiyum kīḻē patti 42 aiyum kāṇka.
40. kimu 1000 - kimu. 300 kālakaṭṭamē tamiḻakattiṉ irumpukkālam allatu peruṅkaṟpaṭic ciṉṉak (Megalithic) kālam eṉṟu kuṟippiṭap paṭukiṟatu. akkāla kaṭṭattil iṟanta varkaḷ tāḻiyil putikkap paṭṭaṉar. kaṟpatukkikaḷil aṭakkam ceyyappaṭṭaṉar. peruṅkaṟpaṭic ciṉṉaṅkaḷ cila viṭaṅkaḷil iruntaṉa; cila viṭaṅkaḷil illi; eṉiṉum ikkālam "peruṅkaṟpaṭic ciṉṉakkālam" eṉṟē potuvāka kuṟippiṭappaṭukiṟatu.
41. ki.mu. 1000 aḷavil makkaḷ perumaḷavukku āṭumāṭu vaḷarppavarkaḷākavē iruntaṉar. vēḷāṇmi toṭakka niliyil iruntirukka vēṇṭum eṉpatu akaḻvāyvukaḷiṉ muṭivākum. koṭumaṇal, tāṇṭikkuṭi pāṉi ōṭṭut tamiḻiṉ poṟippuk kālam ki.mu. 5 allatu 4 ām nūṟṟāṇṭākattāṉ irukka vēṇṭum eṉa rājaṉ niṟuviyuḷḷār. appoḻutē eḻuttaṟivu peṟṟa camutāyam tamiḻakattil iruntatu. eṉavē vara lāṟṟuk kālamē tamiḻakattil ki.mu. 500liruntu toṭaṅki yatākak koḷḷa iṭamuṇṭu eṉpār rājaṉ.
42. tamiḻiya moḻi pēcunar kimu 2000kku muṉṉar iruntē tamiḻakattilum teṉṉāṭṭilum iṭiviṭātu vāḻntu vanta maṇṇiṉ mintarē eṉpatiyē tolliyal āyvukaḷ kāṭṭukiṉṟaṉa eṉpati ālciṉ tampatiyar 1988 tam nūlil piṉvarumāṟu kūṟuvar.
"teṉṉintiyā iṉṟu tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ vaḻaṅkum pakuti. am moḻikaḷip pēcuvōr pūrvīkam eṉṉavāka iruntirukkalām? avarkaḷ (vaṭamēṟkiliruntu) teṉṉintiyā viṟku vantu kuṭiyēṟumpōtu tammuṭaṉ irumpiyum peruṅ kaṟpaṭi nākarikattiyum koṇarntaṉar eṉa (valuvāṉa ātāramiṉṟik kūṟappaṭukiṟatu. tolliyal cāṉṟukaḷiṉpaṭi ippaṭi naṭantirukka vāyppu mikak kuṟivu. irumpup payaṉ pāṭṭiṟku 1000 āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar iruntē karunāṭakat til (teṉ intiyāvil piṟa pakutikaḷilum kūṭa irukkalām). peruṅkaṟpaṭi nākarikak kuṭiyiruppukaḷ iruntuḷḷaṉa; avi iṭiyīṭu iṉṟi toṭarntu iruntu vantuḷḷaṉa ; viyakkat takka paṇpāṭṭut toṭarcciyuṭaṉ iṉṟum uḷḷa paṇ pāṭṭuk kūṟukaḷ pala antap paḻiya nākarikak kūṟukaḷiṉ toṭarcciyākavē tōṉṟu kiṉṟaṉa. uṭaṟkūṟu amippilum antap paḻiya nākarika makkaḷum iṉṟiya makkaḷum perum aḷavukku oṉṟu pōlavē uḷḷaṉar. eṉavē teṉ ṉintiyāvil (pallāyiram āṇṭukaḷukku muṉṉar iruntē ) putukkaṟkāla nākarikam paṭitta makkaḷiṭiyē uruvāṉaviyē tirāviṭa moḻikaḷ eṉpati maṟuppatu kaṭiṉamāka uḷḷatu. teṉṉintiyāvil paṟpala pakutikaḷil irunta putukkaṟkāla nākarika makkaḷiṭiyē toṭarpu iruntirukkum; potuvāṉa tirāviṭa moḻiyum iruntirukkum. ki.mu. 3000 - 1000 kāla aḷavil teṉṉintiyāvil irunta putuk kaṟkālak kuṭiyiruppu kaḷil iruntu piṟantaviyē varalāṟṟuk kālat toṭak kattic cārnta kuṭiyiruppukaḷum eṉalām.''
The southern part of the peninsula is today the homeland of the Dravidian language, and we may well enquire what - speaking in broadest terms - is likely to have been their history. It has been climed, though not on very solid grounds, that the earliest speakers of these languages brought with them into Peninsular India both iron and the custom of making megalithic graves. In the light of archeological evidence this appears to be extremely improbable. We now know that at least for a millennium prior to the arrival of iron, there were established settlements in Karnataka, and probably also in other parts of the peninsula, and these settlements show evidence of a remarkable continuity of culture. Many modern culture trits appear to derive from them, and a substantial part of the populations shows physical affiliation to the Neolithic people. In the light of all this it is difficult to believe that the Dravidian languages do not owe their origin to the same people who produced the Neolithic culture there. ..... the several regional cultures .... had throughout a degree of interaction and probably originally a common language family, Dravidian. ..... the settlements of the third - second millennia appear to be ancestral to those which we encounter there from the beginnings of history onwards. (p. 353) 
peruṅkaṟpaṭi nākarikam tamiḻnāṭṭiliruntē vaṭakkē takkāṇattiṟkup paraviyatu eṉṟu tolliyalaṟiñar ē. cattiyamūrtti 26.5.2007 avarkaḷum intu nāḷitaḻil terivittuḷḷār.

4. pāvāṇar tamatu vaṭamoḻi varalāṟu (1967) nūliṉ pakkaṅkaḷ 63 - 269l "vaṭamoḻip pukunta teṉ coṟkaḷ" mēlum ērāḷamāṉavaṟṟiṉ paṭṭiyalit tantuḷḷār – parō emeṉō pōṉṟavar ēṟkeṉavē kaṇṭa coṟkaḷ cilavum avaṟṟukkum mēlāka pāvāṇar kaṇṭa coṟkaḷ palavumāka) teṉcol mūlat tiricoṟkaḷ, teṉ collaṭip puṇarppuc coṟkaḷ, tamiḻiliruntu moḻipeyarttuk koṇṭa coṟkaḷ, irupiṟappikaḷ (hybrids); vaṭavar pōliyāṉa cams kiruta vēr kāṭṭum coṟkaḷ ; palvakit tirippukaḷāl ''vaṭacoṟkaḷ'' eṉac cātitta coṟkaḷ; tamiḻc cuṭṭuc coṟkaḷ / viṉāc coṟkaḷip piṉpaṟṟi vaṭamoḻiyil uruvāṉa coṟkaḷ ; tamiḻip piṉpaṟṟi vaṭamoḻiyil uruvākkiya puṇarcciyilakkaṇam mutaliyavi ākiyavi paṟṟi annūliṉ pakkaṅkaḷ 269 - 321 l kāṇalām.
4. tamiḻiya moḻikaḷiliruntu vētamoḻi, camskirutam vaṭa intiya (intō āriya moḻik kuṭumpattiṉavākak karutappaṭum) moḻikaḷ ākiyavaṟṟil ēṟiyuḷḷa oliyaṉiyal, ilakkaṇaviyal kūṟukaḷ palavaṟṟi emaṉō tamatu 1956 kaṭṭuriyil niṟuviṉār.
(1) oliyaṉiyal
ṭa; ḷa (rikvētattil), ṇa ākiya vaḷināvolikaḷ (retroflex/ cerebral/domal tamiḻiya moḻikaḷiliruntu kaṭaṉ peṟṟavi. avvolikaḷik koṇṭa coṟkaḷit tamiḻil liruntu kaṭaṉ peṟṟa piṉ olikaḷiyum vaṇṇamāliyil cērtta ṉar.
(ii) viṉiyeccam (gerund/absolutive/incomplete verb/ Past nonfinite verb/ converb / indeclinable Participle/ adverbial Participle/ Past participle (Jothimuthu) / conjunctive Participle (Grierson) rik vēta kālattilēyē ēṟiviṭṭatu.
(iii) nēr kūṟṟu muṭintavuṭaṉ " eṉa/ eṉṟu tamiḻil varuvatu pōl ("taṉ ceyviṉi muṭitteṉak kēṭpal - puṟam 27:9-10) camaskirutattil kūṟṟukkup piṉṉar "iti" iti (= ippaṭik kūṟiṉāṉ) eṉa varutal
(iv) etirolic coṟkaḷ (echo words) - "pulikili'' pōṉṟavi
(v) olikkuṟippuc coṟkaḷ - caḷacaḷ, paṭapaṭa pōṉṟavi
(vi) ōṭu, ōṭṭu, ōṭṭuvi; naṭa, naṭattu, naṭattuvi pōṉṟa, vāypāṭṭu (simplex, eausative, cusative of cusative) viṉi vaṭivaṅkaḷ (vii) emeṉō 1974 kaṭṭuri : tamiḻ "um" mip payaṉ pāṭukaḷip pōṉṟa camaskiruta api api ai payaṉpaṭuttutal
(viii) mēlatu kaṭṭuri: tirāviṭa uṟavu muṟiccoṟ kaḷilum cari intō āriya uṟavu muṟic coṟkaḷilum perumpālum toḻilik kuṟikkum cātip peyar āṇukku uḷḷatu; āṉāl peṇṇik kuṟikkum poḻutu intac cātikkāraṉuṭiya makaḷ maṉivi tāy/piṟa uṟavu eṉṟa aḷavilēyē amintu viṭukiṟatu.
